## E165 Bounded Exhaustive Recovery Replay and Mutation Kill Bound —— Segment 1 has been run (2026-10-01), segment 2 has been run (2026-10-01), rerun 1 has been run (2026-10-01), rerun 2 has been run (four segments, 2026-10-02), rerun 3 has been run (three segments: segment 1, 2026-10-02; segments 2 and 3, 2026-10-03), rerun 4 has been run (two segments: segment 1 and segment 2, 2026-10-03), rerun 5 has been run (enough to judge, 2026-10-04: segments 1, 2, 3 and the two steps of segment 5; segment 4 was not run after it was enough to judge), rerun 6 has been run (2026-10-09: r3 profile legal family), rerun 7 has been partly run (2026-10-23: segment 1 has been run, KA replaced by KL, whose roots are placed by the placement formula; the inline tests of segment 2 were not run and are left to the main agent; segment 3 and P1b were not run after it was enough to judge)

The preregistration is `e165-preregistration.md` (section 12, revisions 1–12); the fork sheet is K3–K9 of `m3-formal-r1-forks.md`. The object under test is `replay_journal` in that version of `crates/singlefs-core/src/recovery.rs` (sha256 `f04814901d9521a1f72bf64aae530157c643fe47f3b0a37ac06695feb9418fdb`), not one character changed. The harness is `crates/singlefs-checker-tier/src/bin/e165_replay_bounded_enumeration.rs` (the checked-in harness: it calls `replay_journal`, `JournalRecord::to_bytes` / `parse`, the real CRC, and the real reverse chain; the reader is two disks in memory).

The sections from "Artifacts" through "Mutations" (including "Results copied line by line from the artifacts", "Adversarial family (segment 1; registration revision 9 was written after the draft readings of the adversarial family had been seen)", "Segment 2: mutation kill bound", and "Per-line verdicts") record the run from before prefix-taking was moved into the core. The object under test is that copy of `recovery.rs` with sha256 `f04814901d9521a1f72bf64aae530157c643fe47f3b0a37ac06695feb9418fdb`. The rerun after the move is in "Rerun 1 (after prefix-taking was moved into the core)". The readings that differ are rows 448, 613, and 1004 of the segment-2 table.

Artifacts:

- `research/results/e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-01-light-legal-family.out`: `--bounds light --family legal`, 876 lines, 6 923 358 states.

- `research/results/e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-01-segment1-adversarial-family.out`: `--bounds segment1 --family adversarial`, 172 lines, 64 513 748 states (run after registration revision 9 narrowed halt clause H4).
- `e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-01-segment1-adversarial-family-timing.out`: elapsed-time lines and `uptime` of the two adversarial-family runs (16 threads, 7 threads). They are not in the byte-for-byte comparison.
- `e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-01-segment2-kill-bounds.out`: segment 2, 26 variants (baseline 1, 21 distinct replacements covering 23 rows in `crates/mutations.tsv`, 4 hand-made rows), one or two lines each, 32 lines.
- `research/results/e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-01-segment2-red-examples.tsv`: the red-example ledger of segment 2, 151 lines (one counterexample state per red variant, per red domain, at the first red N). The format is in its header.
- `e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-01-segment2-kill-bounds-timing.out`: timing, load, and the one single-variant measurement of the segment-2 driver. They are not in the byte-for-byte comparison.

### What this segment did

- **Legal-history family** (L of registration 5.2, L small ring 6 and 9, U verification on and off, C big ring and small ring 6, X, e1, e2, R): the generator builds legal histories from the clauses cited by settled item 4 and settled item 14 of D23 (Journal Role & Format) and settled item 1 of D13 (Verification Route). It exhausts how many records each publish writes, how the commit marks are arranged, and every on-disk subset of the records. Each state calls `replay_journal` once. Criteria j0–j4 and j6, handwritten from the clause text, and implementation anchors IA1 and IA2 each count their own violations. Domain C also counts J5a (applied-set reading) and J5b (landed-version reading) on every state pair s ⊆ u. The five pair-sets are each reported on their own, side by side with "ideal recovery" (landing on the farthest version j\* written per the clauses).
- **Adversarial family** (A, A small ring 6, A chain): the first run stopped on registration halt clause H4 (the alphabet in registration 5.2 includes sequence number 0; `crates/singlefs-core/src/journal.rs` says "sequence numbers start at 1 (settled item 4): reading a 0 counts this record as damaged"; `parse` rejects it; revision 7). The main agent wrote revision 9: a record whose sequence number is not 0 is asserted to decode under the original wording of H4; a record whose sequence number is 0 is asserted to be rejected by `parse`, and the run stops if it is not. **Revision 9 was written after the draft readings of the adversarial family had been seen.** After the harness changed its assertions to match, the official run was checked in, and the new artifact's `E7RESULT` lines are byte-for-byte the same as that draft. A record with sequence number 0 cannot enter the table, so S's (e) "sequence number 0" cannot be reached on the tables that were built (revision 7).
- **Segment 2** (registration 5.5 and 5.6, revisions 10–12): the driver, in each variant's own copy, edits `crates/singlefs-core/src/recovery.rs`, cold-builds the harness, and runs `--bounds segment2`, comparing each (domain, N) with the baseline. The bound is fixed at the upper edge of 5.6 (revision 10 removed the relaxation). Each domain's "first red N" is the first N that differs from the baseline. A red domain looks for one counterexample state at the first red N, and the original implementation and the variant binary each replay that one state once.
- The harness gained two parameters for segment 2 (revision 12 allows them; not one criterion was changed): `--replay-state <domain>:<N>:<ordinal>` judges only that one state in enumeration order, and `--list-states <domain>:<N>` prints one line per state. Both are single-threaded and do not run the self-checks or the positive controls. After they were added, the whole legal-history family of segment 1 was rerun, and the `E7RESULT` lines match the checked-in copy byte for byte.
- In the order of registration 5.5: ① constant cross-check assertions and 13 harness self-checks; ② positive controls (35 readings of the legal-history family, 10 of the adversarial family); ③–⑥ each domain.

```
59:E7RESULT name=violations domain=A ring_slots=64 n=3 criterion=AS_consecutive_gap_continues violations=1024 judged=778034 witness=anchor=absent;slots=t+1:o1:L0:C0:x1:chain_true,t+0:o1:L1:C0:x1:chain_true,t+1:o1:L1:C1:x1:chain_true;first=returned:txg100:watermark1000:applied0;expected=txg101:watermark9003038/txg101:watermark9003038/txg101:watermark9003038
```

- The three readings separate pairwise (the three A-mutual counts are non-zero in all three domains), and the three AS counts are non-zero in the same domain. Witness on line 59: slot 2 has the same txg as the chosen root and carries the last-record flag; S takes it as the anchor and applies slot 3; the implementation's `above` orders by counter, the first is slot 1 (txg t + 1), and it stops when that does not equal the expected counter, applying nothing. This is reached only when a record above the watermark has a counter smaller than the anchor (within the same instance, txg rollback follows the counter).
- A-structure and A-limit are 0 in all three domains: once the premise is included, the root the implementation returns is either the chosen root or is taken from a record in the same instance with a larger txg, a commit mark, and the last-record flag, and the reported applied count does not exceed the in-flight limit.
- Violations where the j6 premise holds (A from 464, witness on line 63): in the version the first call landed on, the table has two records with the same txg and the last-record flag, and the second call reports `more_than_one_last_record_flag`.
- The count of states that hold at least one record rejected by `parse` (sequence number 0) is reported as `states_holding_a_record_rejected_by_parse`: 45 815 520 of the 57 585 320 states in domain A.

- "Red", on the measurement basis of registration 5.5, means "different from the baseline", with no direction. Column 7 of the red-example ledger carries a direction (`+` is a violation the variant added, `-` is one it dropped), and only for the first-red state, not for the domain total. Several "red" cells are the variant having fewer violations than the baseline: j1–j4 in domain R (the 2026-10-01 implementation lands outside the timeline on a residue state; a variant that applies nothing, or applies less, does not land there; the counterexample states of tsv-24, 512, 920, 921, 922, 1004, and Z1 are all `-J1`), j6 premise holds on e1 (tsv-24, 512, 517, 921, 922, and Z1 are `-J6_premise_holds`), and j6 premise fails on the small ring for Z2 and Z4 (`-J6_premise_fails`). tsv-406 (same as 414) is `+J6_premise_holds` on e1 at N = 3.
- The red-example ledger has 151 lines. The original implementation and the variant binary each replay one state, and 151 lines have the same verdict as the per-state line: `awk -F'\t' '!/^#/{t++; if($8=="true"&&$9=="true")c++} END{print t,c}'` prints `151 151`.
- Table A of registration section 9, "predicted sampling point that changes the output first", was checked line by line: every row is red at the (domain, N) it predicted, and the criterion name matches. Most variants also go red earlier in another domain (for example tsv-406 in domain C at Nc = 2). tsv-1004 is caught first on e1 at N = 1 by the implementation anchor "count of error states in the legal-history family". Prediction 7 (448 may be not red in every domain) and prediction 8 (226 is only IA2) were measured.

### Per-line verdicts

| Registered quantity | Reading (legal-history family) | Recorded as what, per registration sections 6 and 10 |

| Q1 original implementation j1 (returned root) | L, L6, L9, U verification on, X, e1, C, and c6 are all 0 | F1 did not fire: within bounds the original implementation passes M2's "returned root" reading in the legal-history domain |
| Q2 original implementation j2 (root plus disk) | the same domains are all 0 | the original-implementation half: j1 and j2 are 0 in every domain together, so the two readings do not separate within bounds (the first half of F2; the second half needs the mutations of segment 2) |
| Q3 j3 (in-flight limit) | big ring, 6 slots, and 9 slots are all 0 | the original implementation passes the limit sentence of I-8.3 (Strictly Continuous Replay Prefix) |
| Q4 j4 (farthest version) | L, L6, L9, U verification on, X, e1, C, and c6 are all 0 | F6 did not fire: the "apply" question is true within bounds against the 2026-10-01 implementation |
| Q5 j0 and the count of error states | j0 is all 0; the only non-zero count of error states is e2's 42 (all 42 states error, which is IA1) | the legal-history family (L, L small ring, U verification on, X, e1) has 0 error states, matching the class-A anchors of registration section 7, and F11 did not fire |
| Q6 J5a, J5b | J5b is 0 on all five pair-sets and both rings; J5a is non-zero on "wide", "cut A legal", "cut A same-segment", "cut B legal", and "cut B same-segment" (big ring 830 685 / 4 969 / 911 / 8 146 / 911); all 911 of cut A same-segment are on the "stopped in the root-slot segment" row, and "stopped in the record segment" is 0; ideal recovery and the original implementation match cell by cell | violations of the applied-set reading are a property of the reading, not of the implementation: any recovery that lands on the farthest version has them. Which set counts as "inside the segment" is for the main agent to judge; whichever one is chosen, J5a is non-zero and J5b is 0 |
| Q7 j6 (fixed point) | premise holds: L, L6, L9, U, C, c6, and X are all 0, and e1 is 70 (the second call errors on the root whose txg is at the top); premise fails: the big ring has not one state (the limit cannot bite), L6 354 / 1 272, L9 51 998 / 557 792, c6 215 / 596 | F8 fires on e1: the fixed-point reading is also false when the premise is included. The witness is that the first call lands on the version whose txg equals the maximum u64, and the second call reports `CHOSEN_ROOT_CHECKPOINT_TXG_HAS_NO_SUCCESSOR`. F9 did not fire: on the small ring, j6 is non-zero among states where the premise fails, so the premise carries the weight. It flips with the geometry: the cell where the premise fails has no states on the big ring and is non-zero on the small ring; both points are written down |
| Q8 named verification turned off | U verification off: j1 is 0, j2 is 6 726 648, j4 is 6 726 648, IA2 is 0 | F12 fires on the "root plus disk" reading and does not fire on the "returned root" reading: the strategy of turning off named verification itself passes j1 and fails j2 |
| Q11 domain R | j1 is 956 (out of 6 964 states), witness N = 2 | F17 did not fire: without the premise "one (instance generation, counter) corresponds to only one content", the literal wording of M2 is false, so the premise carries the weight |
| Q12 domains non-empty | every domain has `domain_is_nonempty=true` (e2 is excepted, as the registration does not require it); in domain C the number of states whose chosen root is not R_0 is 115 520 on the big ring at N = 6 | V4 did not fire |
| Q9 the three A-mutual counts and the three AS counts | the three A-mutual counts are non-zero on A, a6, and AC (A: 175 616 / 509 216 / 333 600); the three AS counts are non-zero in all three domains (A: 330 240 / 154 624 / 488 224) | F13 did not fire: the three readings separate within bounds. F14 fires: the 2026-10-01 implementation matches none of the three readings (all three counts are non-zero in the same domain) |
| Q10 A-structure, A-limit | all 0 in the three domains | F16 did not fire: after the premise is included, the original implementation passes both the structure clause and the limit clause in the adversarial domain |
| Q13 kill bound of the mutation table (25 variants) | clause criteria red on 23; caught only by an implementation anchor on 1 (table row 226, IA2 on U verification off at N = 2); not red within bounds on 1 (table row 448, every domain matches the baseline line by line up to the upper edge); invalid 0 | F18 fires: row 448 is not red within bounds; row 226 is listed separately as "clause criteria not red within bounds". F19 does not apply (revision 10 removed the relaxation). F15 fires: rows 613, 614, 514, and 448 are not red within bounds in every domain of the legal-history family; of these, 613 (A, n = 1), 614, and 514 (A, n = 2) are red in the adversarial family, and 448 is red in neither family |
| Q13 the four K3 rows | rows 518, 406, 414, and 36: j2 is red on L, L6, U, C, X, and e1 (N = 2–4; row 36 is not red on e1 up to the upper edge), and j1 is not red at the same N; j1 is red only in domain R (518, 406, 414, and 36 are all N = 3) | F2 did not fire: the first red N of j1 and of j2 differ on the legal-history domains, so they separate within bounds. F3 did not fire: these rows are red on j1 in domain R. Note: once a domain is red the scan does not go higher, so whether j1 is red at a larger N in these domains was not measured |
| Q13 K5's Z1 | On L, L6, U verification on, and X (N = 2–3), Z1 differs from the baseline only on j4; on R (N = 2) j1, j2, and j3 differ; on U verification off (N = 2) j2 differs; on C and c6 (Nc = 1) J5a differs; on e1 (N = 2) j6 premise-holds differs; on A, a6, and AC (n = 1) the three AS counts differ | On the literal wording of the registration, "different from the baseline", F5 fires (besides j4 it is also red on j1, j2, j3, J5a, and j6). On the counterexample states in the red-example ledger these differences are all the variant having fewer violations (R's `-J1`, e1's `-J6_premise_holds`). The harness did not report the direction of J5a in domain C. Reading `judge_a_legal_state`, the inference is that the variant always lands on the chosen root, the applied set is empty, and the J5a violations become 0 (this was not measured). Read as "extra violations", only j4 is red in the legal-history family, which is the shape of F4. Which reading to take is for the main agent to decide |
| Q14 whether j6 separates the mutation table | Rows 406 and 414 add a j6 premise-holds violation on e1 at N = 3 (counterexample state `+J6_premise_holds`); rows 24, 512, 517, 921, and 922 and Z1 drop a j6 violation on the e1 counterexample state; Z2 and Z4 drop a j6 premise-fails violation on the small-ring counterexample state (all of these look only at the one first-red state, not at the domain total) | F10 did not fire: some mutations are red on j6, and 406 and 414 are extra violations |

Of predictions 1–11, what segment 1 could measure: prediction 1 (the applied-set reading is false on the pair "records all landed, root slot landed or not") was measured (domain C, Nc = 1, 1 of 9 pairs in the "wide" set); prediction 2 (the fixed point is false on the small ring when the premise fails) was measured; prediction 3 (with verification off it passes "returned root" and fails "root plus disk") was measured; prediction 9 (it fails "returned root" in the residue domain) was measured. One thing the registration did not predict: 70 j6 violations on e1 where the premise holds.

### Assertions that pin absolute values, and positive controls

- Class-B anchors of registration section 7 (the state count of each domain at each N, the witness counts of domain L at N ≤ 7, the pair counts and legal crash-state counts of domain C, including the two small-ring groups added in section 12) are `assert_eq!` cell by cell in the harness. One cell that does not match exits the process on the spot. Both artifacts reached `name=done`.
- S's own cross-check (registration 5.4, void clause V3): the count of states in L, L6, L9, U verification on, X, and e1 where the expected placement of S's three readings differs from j\*. All 123 `name=specification_self_check` lines are `mismatches=0`.
- All 35 positive-control readings are red (`controls_not_red=0`): j0 is 1, j1 is 762, j4 is 762 (each equal to the number of states in the control bound with j\* ≥ 1, the sum of the witness counts for N = 1..5 in registration section 7), j2 is 1 032 while j1 is 0, j3 is 354 on the small ring and 0 on the big ring, J5a is 225, J5b is 35, J5a and J5b are equal line by line on the nine pair-set rows, j6 is 3 110, IA1 is 42, IA2 is 2 058.
- The legal crash-state count of domain C names the cut (the `name=legal_crash_states` lines of artifact `research/results/e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-01-segment1-legal-family.out`, N summed line by line): cut A (`cut_own`, the root-slot write is a segment of its own, the barrier position of D16 (Publish Semantics) settled item 7) is 8 006 in domain C and 383 in domain c6, 8 389 together; cut B (`cut_with`, the root-slot write is in the same segment as the records) is 14 737 in domain C and 606 in domain c6, 15 343 together. The 15 343 cited in the three-party verdict is cut B's number. Citing the legal-state count of domain C must name the cut (three-party verdict `research/prompts/m3-formal-r3-main-verification.md`, section 5, item 1).

### Sensitivity to geometry

| Knob | Sampling point | Whether the verdict flips |
|---|---|---|
| Ring slot count (in-flight limit) | 64 slots (limit 21), 9 slots (3), 6 slots (2) | j1–j4 are 0 at all three points, no flip; the cell where the j6 premise fails has no states on the big ring and is non-zero on the small ring, a flip; J5a and J5b have the same verdict on the big ring and on the 6-slot small ring |
| Starting counter of the history | big ring 7 (no wrap), small ring equal to the ring slot count (wrap in the middle of the history) | it follows the ring; j1–j4 do not flip |
| txg of the chosen root | 100, maximum u64 − 1, maximum u64 | the cell where the j6 premise holds flips to non-zero at maximum u64 − 1 |

Distribution of the in-flight limit: in the big-ring cells the largest number of records above the chosen root is 9 (domain L, N = 10), and the number of states above the limit is 0. The limit cannot bite on the big ring. That is not evidence that the fixed point holds on the big ring. Whether the third call equals the second on states where j6 is false (reported, not judged): on L6 at N = 6, 210 equal and 62 differ; on L9 at N = 9, 33 904 equal and 7 600 differ.

Knobs not swept: disk count (two disks, records only on disk 0), record byte count and F, the values of the instance generation, and how many units one record names.

### `light` tier

On 2026-10-01 the user settled this harness as a lightweight tool. Bounds of `--bounds light`: L up to 9, U up to 5, A up to 3, and the other domains the same as segment 1. How they were chosen: a draft trial measured 0.24–1.2 microseconds of wall-clock time per state at 10 threads, and each domain was taken to stay within 3 seconds. What was checked in is the `--family legal` copy (6 923 358 states, 2.671 seconds on the process-monotonic clock). The state counts and witness counts are pinned to the same constant table as segment 1. While measuring, another session on the same machine was running a 31-thread broad mutation and a solver. The three load averages `uptime` reported before the run started are 10.19, 29.98, and 34.04 (raw output in the first two lines of the timing artifact). Segment 1 `--family legal` took 29.061 seconds under the same load.

### Mutations

19 unit tests (`grep -c '#\[test\]' crates/singlefs-checker-tier/src/bin/e165_replay_bounded_enumeration.rs`; the two added for segment 2 cover the single-state replay parameters and the shape of the per-state line; the three added with the batch that reads the pin table cover `--pinned-cases`: a line must have eight fields, the domain and the size must be in range, and the input fingerprint recomputed from the pinned state line must match the copy in the table, reporting drift when it differs). 20 harness mutations are in `crates/mutations.tsv`, with names starting with "E165 (Bounded Exhaustive Recovery Replay and Mutation Kill Bound) " (`grep -c '^[E]165 ' crates/mutations.tsv`). Neither the unit tests nor the mutations were run: tests of the checker tier package belong to the gate checker-tier-crates-mutation-replay at commit time. Each harness run starts with 13 self-checks, and each of those calls the same function as one unit test. In the three segment-1 artifacts, all 13 `name=self_check` lines are `passed=true`.

The mutation table measured in segment 2 is table A of registration section 9 (the 23 rows in `crates/mutations.tsv` that touch the five functions of `replay_journal`, plus Z1–Z4 of the hand-made table `scripts/mutation/tables/e165_replay_bounded_enumeration_custom.tsv`). Before the driver started it re-selected by the registration's method, and it ran only when the row numbers matched the registration.

### Rerun 1 (after prefix-taking was moved into the core)

Question sheet `e165-r1-questions.md` (R1–R3). Rerun preregistration `research/prompts/e165-r1-prereg.md` (including revision 1 and revision 2). Why the rerun: the prefix-taking judgment moved from `crates/singlefs-core/src/recovery.rs` into `crates/singlefs-replay-prefix/src/lib.rs`, and the glue stayed in `replay_journal` of `recovery.rs` (batches 3–5 of the milestone-three formal-verification landing). The conclusion holds for the tree of the frozen copy of code round m3-verus-land-code-r3 (file list `research/prompts/m3-verus-land-code-r3-snapshot/crates-sha256.txt`, 217 files; sha256 of `recovery.rs` is `40067ae38fc76d7945ac61ee088682d6ce5ef578955c80f3268316e7597dd504`, of `lib.rs` is `5f29497341979dba2aad7befe067f117d9b207392da64d7e76651d3a63514190`, on the `name=restored` line of `e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-01-r1-segment2-kill-bounds.out`). The main workspace's `crates/` on 2026-10-01 already differs from that list. The rerun was not done on the main workspace's tree.

**R1 is the same.** Segment 1 was rerun on a copy of the frozen copy (artifact prefix `r1-segment1b-`): `--bounds segment1`, the legal-history family and the adversarial family each run twice, at 10 threads and at 5 threads. All four copies are byte-for-byte the same as the checked-in copy: the legal-history family is 910 lines, sha256 `eb54d5032d53d93b74bd0febdb60d1124c0bfd7f3d2750cf084f856dacd5b3c5`; the adversarial family is 172 lines, sha256 `3ca19987eed1d99dfe2987e1c1f7a336fc98764f9fb936a4f00d7c919ee3da6e`. The positive control (take the middle line of the file and add one to the number after `violations=`) reports "different" for both families, and the first difference is exactly the line that was changed (line 455 of the legal-history family, line 86 of the adversarial family). Comparing an original with itself reports "same" for each. At the start and the end of the segment, `sha256sum -c` on the copy's root is 217 OK and 0 not OK, and there are 0 files under `crates/` outside the list. The comparison is in `e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-01-r1-segment1b-comparison.out`. The "not OK" counts on its lines 3 and 4 are a script-escaping mistake (they took the total line count). A correction was appended at the end of the file.

The first pass of segment 1 (artifact prefix `r1-segment1-`) is void: during the segment another session changed three files in the main workspace's `crates/` (`crates/mutations.tsv`, `crates/singlefs-checker-tier/src/bin/new_pool_file_creation_on_device.rs`, `crates/singlefs-harness/tests/entries_after_mount_refuse_swapped_or_behind_devices.rs`). At the end of the segment `sha256sum -c` on the list is 214 OK and 3 FAILED, so void clause RV6 voids it. That pass's comparison itself is also the same (both families' sha256 match the second pass), but it is not accepted. The nine artifacts are kept as they are.

**R2 is not the same set.** The `name=verdict` line in `e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-01-r1-segment2-row-selection.out` (the line number at the start is the artifact's own line number):


- "What the second call reports" was **not taken as the original `what`** in either shape (the red-example ledger stores only the per-state line, and the variant binary is deleted when the run finishes). It is inferred: j6 recorded a violation and J6Qn did not, and ρ_1's txg is the maximum u64, so under J6Qn (ii) the only branch that separates the two criteria is "the second call reports `UnitMalformed` and `what` equals `CHOSEN_ROOT_CHECKPOINT_TXG_HAS_NO_SUCCESSOR`". This is not the kind registration 5.2 calls "Qn judges some other error as equal" (that kind would need ρ_1's txg not at the top while a second-call error is still judged equal; in the nine cells, every state judged equal by (ii) has ρ_1's txg at the maximum u64, and the top was checked by the criterion itself). The variant defect in all nine cells is caught by j4 on the same (e1, N) (shape 2 also has j2). Whether to record it separately under SF3 is for the main agent.

The four domain-R rows are the `n=2` through `n=5` lines of `name=table_comparison domain=R ring_slots=64` in `reversed-disk-order-legal-family.out`.) Discriminative power: the two positive controls T-table and T-row are fully red on 3 readings each in every `--table both` artifact (domain L, N ≤ 5: `tables_differ` 2 989 = `direct_table_holds_a_record` 2 989, `rows_differ` 596 > 0; `rows_differ` 3 110 = state count 3 110, `tables_differ` 0). After the rows added in segment 4 are removed, `--table both` matches the default r2 profile output of the same family line by line (all three `r2_table_both_profile_check` lines of `profile-check.out` are `identical_after_removing_the_new_rows=true`). The two passes of the multi-disk family, at 8 threads and at 4 threads, are byte-for-byte the same in full. The assertion on the scanned disk-table entry count of the state "every record landed on every disk" was actually reached at every (domain, N) of every non-adversarial domain (`fully_landed_scan_table_size_checked` greater than 0). Verdict lines: the 6 `--table both` and disk-order artifacts have 0 `name=void` lines and 0 `name=halt` lines. The "did not pass" counts in the verdict lines (j6 premise fails / premise holds, J5a, the adversarial family's AS series, domain R's j1–j4 at 956, `tables_differ` 3 482, and `rows_differ` 1 344) are all numbers expected by the registration or already reported in earlier segments (`grep -n 'name=verdict' <artifact>`, read line by line; there is no `false` or `not_run` field).

The fix to the startup self-check (the cause of the 5 variant panics in segment 2): the three items in the r2 profile startup self-check that read implementation outcomes (`j6qn_control_readings`, `j7_control_readings`, `odd_marker_parity_turns_the_unmount_marker_controls`) were moved into unit tests. Not one character of the assertions was changed, and the `name=control` lines are still reported. After the fix, on a copy of the frozen root, each of the 5 segment2b variants was built once and run on the r2 profile at a small bound (a draft, not checked in): all 5 passed the 15 startup self-checks and finished (exit 1: the J6Qn positive control is not red under the variant, recorded as V2), and none panicked. **The harness after the move therefore cannot reproduce the r2 artifacts from before the move.** Compared in full, the only differences are the three `name=self_check` lines and the counts in the closing line. The three `r2_profile_check` lines of `profile-check.out` are `identical_apart_from_the_three_moved_self_check_rows_and_their_count_in_the_run_verdict_and_done=true`. The same-command rerun after the move left three new files (names carrying a `-2026-10-02` suffix).

### Rerun 3 (combined judgment of root selection and replay, variants that break the root selection order, J6Qn split by "txg of journal records in the same instance does not decrease with the counter")

Question sheet `e165-r3-questions.md` (Q1, Q2, Q3). Rerun preregistration `research/prompts/e165-r3-prereg.md` (including revisions 1 through 3). Status: **has been run (three segments)**. Segment 1 (Q3, the adversarial-family J6Qn "premise holds" row split into two rows by that predicate), segment 2 (Q1, real `choose_root` selects the root and then real `replay_journal` is called, and whether a confirmed complete publish is lost), and segment 3 (Q2, whether the four variants that break the root selection order have discriminative power; driver r3 mode, registration revision 3) have all been run, and all three rows of the question sheet have been judged. The harness unit tests (56, 4 added in segment 1 and 9 added in segment 2; segment 3 did not touch the harness) and the harness mutations in `crates/mutations.tsv` (8 rows in segment 1, 11 rows in segment 2) belong to the commit-time run. The conclusions of these three segments are marked "unit tests and harness mutations not run".

The object under test is still `replay_journal` (the same record table, the same in-memory disk, the same reader called twice, the second call taking the root returned by the first as the chosen root). The only addition is the split: "txg of journal records in the same instance does not decrease with the counter" (project number I-8.12 (Non-Decreasing Journal Record TXG for Same Instance) in the dispatch record; not yet entered in `crates/singlefs-checker/invariants/03-journal.md`; the definition, in full, is line 9 of section 2 of `research/prompts/m3-inv-b-r1-main-verification.md`) is judged on "the record table this state hands to `replay_journal`": the table is laid out in ascending order of (instance, counter), and it fails when, within the same instance, the later of two adjacent records has a smaller txg than the earlier one. Sequence number 0, rejected by `parse`, cannot enter the table and is not looked at. In the harness this is the local spelling `record_table_satisfies_i812`. It does not call the Verus precondition `journal_txg_does_not_decrease_with_the_counter_within_an_instance` in `crates/` (the argument is the same table handed to the core).

All of rerun 2's 85 400 (A) and 2 904 (AC) fall on the row "premise holds · predicate does not hold": A n = 3 is 464, n = 4 is 84 936, AC n = 3 is 2 904, and a6 is all 0. The artifacts were produced by the release binary built on the segment-1 frozen root (a copy of the main workspace, 233 files, list sha256 `96dfb902556f04c29fbfcbc06050315217133c96560ad775edc91f877289f1dc`, `sha256sum -c` exits 0 at the start and the end of the segment). **What it cannot answer (rerun 3, segment 1)**: Q3 only answers "which side of that predicate the batch of violations from rerun 2 falls on". The predicate-holds row being 0 is not a Verus proof, and it is not the spec matching the executable code. It only says that none of these 64 513 748 adversarial states is a state where "the predicate holds and the premise holds" and the second call departs from the first (outside the bound, and other alphabets, were not swept). The predicate is judged on the table handed to the core (sequence number 0 rejected by `parse` does not enter the table). The reading that instead judges "every slot on disk" was not measured (registration 9.2 enters it as one mutation, and no number was reported). Q1 and Q2 have no numbers.

#### Rerun 3, segment 2 (Q1: combined judgment of root selection and replay, 2026-10-03)

Revision 2 of preregistration `research/prompts/e165-r3-prereg.md` (written before the segment-2 artifacts, on the basis of trial-run readings, not checked in, of the harness on the light tier and on domain K at `--max-u 3`). The harness adds `--family root_choice` (only under `--profile r3`; `--family all` does not include it). The readers and the output of the r1 and r2 profiles are not changed by one character. The object under test is the three steps chained in the `Consult` arm of `recover`: `choose_root`, `scan_journal`, and `replay_journal(…, true)`. `choose_system_configuration` and the glue outside the three steps are not in it. The harness builds a system configuration struct literal from the state's geometry and hands it to the three steps.

- **States**: one state = one in-memory disk with a root ring laid out at the same time (three region memberships 0 / 1 / 0, S slots per region) and a journal ring (two disks mirrored). K (ring of 64 slots, S = 8, 1 or 2 records per publish), K9 (ring of 9 slots, in-flight limit 3), and KW (S = 4, the root ring wraps at txg 12 and covers the generation-0 root planted by mkfs) are legal histories: mkfs plants the generation-0 root, then M mounts, each mount writes the write-row and the warm-up empty publish and then u_m user publishes, crash points cut the segment by device (a publish of k records has 4^k + 1 crash points), and roots and records are built by the generator in persistence order. KA is not a legal history: the first three slots of the root ring in root ring order each take empty or one of four roots with distinct (txg, instance generation), the journal ring is empty, 125 states, and it is only a positive control for the root selection order variants.
- **Criteria** (each reported on its own): version = (`checkpoint_txg`, instance generation), txg primary, instance generation breaking ties. KJ0: the three steps do not panic and `choose_root` hands back one root. KJ1: the landed version is not earlier than the last confirmed user publish before the crash (confirmed = a user publish before the publish the crash point sits in; the write-row and the warm-up empty publish are not the user's fsync). KJ2: not earlier than any self-verified root in the root ring. KJ3: the six projection fields of the landed root equal ideal recovery. When `replay_journal` reports an error, the landed version is taken from the chosen root. Separately, `read_root_ring_slot` is called slot by slot and run as a differential against the truth at generation time.
- **Where the criteria come from**: KJ1 and KJ2 are the two sentences of question-sheet Q1. What counts as confirmed follows D16 (Publish Semantics) settled item 7. The choose-newer order follows D22 (Synthesizing Unit Atomicity) settled item 7 and the version order of M3 in D13 (Verification Route) settled item 1. The chain head and ideal recovery follow note 1 of D23 (Journal Role & Format) settled item 14. Warm-up follows D16 (Publish Semantics) settled item 8. The acceptance of C650 (No Combined Judgment of Root Selection and Replay) is "on a family of states, call real `choose_root` and then call replay, and judge that a confirmed complete publish is not lost".

KJ3 and KJ0 are also 0. The three steps do not panic, `choose_root` does not hand back empty, and `replay_journal` does not report an error. The read-slot differential matches the truth slot by slot. On KA (not a legal history) KJ2 is also 0 (124 states that have a root), and KJ0's 1 is the state whose root ring is entirely empty, the number the registration pins | The four lines `name=verdict domain=K ring_slots=64`, `domain=K9`, `domain=KW`, and `domain=KA`, and the line `name=verdict scope=run`, in `research/results/e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-03-r3-segment2-root-choice.out` (the verdict lines of the four domains and of the whole run, copied in full in the code block below). **What it cannot answer (rerun 3, segment 2)**: Q1's "not dropped" holds only within bounds, on a legal history, without faults: both disks of the root ring and of the journal ring are fully readable, there is no torn root slot, no dropped disk, no device lost and then found again (the shape of C331 (Root selection inversion overrides acknowledged writes)), no administrator rollback and no instance switch, the journal ring does not wrap, and the contents of system configuration (the tail witness, the number-acquisition write) are not in it. There are only two disks, region membership is only 0 / 1 / 0, slots per region are only 4 and 8, the root slot is 512 wide, M ≤ 3, K's U ≤ 4, and each publish has at most 2 records. Outside the bound was not swept. The new-root segment of a zero-unit publish takes the previous version's on the real write path, and here every pair differs. The write-row publish really has to write the instance-table unit and name it, and here it does not name one (the three steps compare only keys and the projection, and do not read them). KA is not a legal history, and the numbers in the KA cell do not overturn Q1. "Confirmed" on the criterion side, ideal recovery, and the independent generator are all written from the same registration text: if the registration text itself is wrong, the harness and the independent generator will be wrong together and still match cell by cell. The disk-reading layer inside the three steps (how many bytes `read_root_ring_slot` reads, and the two gates of fsid and extent offset) was checked only on the harness's reader, not on a real device. The divergence in note 2 of D23 (Journal Role & Format) settled item 14, "root selection takes txg as primary, and the record watermark takes instance generation as primary", cannot be built in the legal histories of this family (the root ring has no two roots of the same txg and different instances). The numbers for Q2 (the four variants that break the root selection order) are in the segment-3 section. The harness unit tests and harness mutations were not run. Until they are run at commit time, the conclusions of these two segments are readings at the artifact layer.

#### Rerun 3, segment 3 (Q2: whether the variants that break the root selection order have discriminative power, 2026-10-03)

Revision 3 of preregistration `research/prompts/e165-r3-prereg.md` (written before the segment-3 artifacts, on the basis of three checks done after the driver r3 mode was written and before the driver was started for a whole run: `--list-items` lists 7 lines, a pure function fed the segment-2 artifact and hand-edited lines, and a fake cargo and a fake harness). This reading has no checked-in output file. The harness was not changed by one character (sha256 is still `acd76c379479d5e9e23bc8a1fd6552c1af4f0108655c2a69c37a4ee3c2304a58`). Segment 3 changed the row in `crates/mutations.tsv` that carries `m3_first_valid_wins` (the original text of that segment-4 row of rerun 2 hits twice in the harness; it was changed to a unique anchor that carries the function header, the meaning is unchanged, and the replacement text was not compiled).

**How it was done**: on the segment-2 frozen root, each of the six variants of registration 9.1 (found verbatim by mutation name in the root's `crates/mutations.tsv`, each exactly 1 row, the original text hitting exactly once, and the hit falling in the range of `choose_root_slot` (`lib.rs`) or `choose_root` (`recovery.rs`)) got its own copy and was cold-built once. The baseline and every variant call the harness `--profile r3 --bounds segment1 --family root_choice` (each binary, one pass of 2 937 251 states), and KJ0 through KJ3 violation counts and the `root_choice_states` count lines are compared per (domain, M, U). The baseline at the start is first compared line by line with the segment-2 artifact (TV7). At the end the baseline is built once more, and the closing line is written only when it is verbatim the same as the start (TV9). The command (from the repository root; baseline at 6 threads, each variant at 3 threads, as the driver sets): `E165_KILL_BOUNDS_MEMORY_CAP=1G bash scripts/hosts/capped.sh 6 bash scripts/experiment-apparatus/e165-replay-mutation-kill-bounds.sh --profile r3 --root <segment-2 frozen root> --scratch <draft directory> --output research/results/e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-03-r3-segment3-root-choice-kill-bounds.out --jobs 2 --threads 3`, exit 0.

| Variant (the text at the start of column 1 of `crates/mutations.tsv`; the row number in the root) | What it changes | Where it sits in Q2 |
|---|---|---|
| 1823 root selection core, comparison ignores the instance generation | on a txg tie, stop at the earlier one in root ring order | Q2's "comparison ignores the instance" |
| 1824 root selection core, take the first valid one | once a self-verified slot is chosen, do not replace it | Q2's "take the first valid one" |
| 1825 root selection core, on a txg tie the lower instance generation wins | tie takes the lower | Q2's "same checkpoint_txg, tie takes the lower" |
| 1826 root selection core, instance generation primary, txg only breaks ties | the primary order of the comparison is reversed | Q2's "instance generation primary" |
| 1822 root selection core, a tie takes the later one as the winner | of two slots whose choose-newer keys are exactly the same, take the later one | incidental (TQ14) |
| 1827 root selection glue, when spreading the choose-newer key, do not carry the instance generation | every instance generation the core sees is 0 | incidental (TQ14) |

| Question | Conclusion | Basis (artifacts, numbers as written) |
|---|---|---|
| Q2: does every variant that breaks the root selection order (comparison ignores the instance, take the first valid one, tie takes the lower, instance generation primary) go red within bounds on Q1's judgment (the KJ1 or KJ2 violation count differs from the baseline on some cell of K, K9, or KW) | **No** (only within bounds, legal, without faults): of the four, only 1824 (take the first valid one) is red. 1823, 1825, and 1826 match the baseline cell by cell on the KJ1 and KJ2 violation counts (and on KJ0 and KJ3) across the 36 cells of K, K9, and KW, and are not red within bounds. All three variants' readings on KA equal the values the registration pins (18, 36, 18, `ka_pin=match`): the variants really did change the root selection order, but the legal history has no root ring that lets it show. This family has no sampling point for them (insensitive to the sampling point, not an equivalent mutation, and not a criterion without discriminative power) | The three lines `item=tsv-1823`, `item=tsv-1825`, and `item=tsv-1826` (the three variants that are not red), the line `item=tsv-1824` (1824), and the `name=verdict` line (the verdict line, copied in full in the code block below) in `research/results/e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-03-r3-segment3-root-choice-kill-bounds.out` |

The baseline (its implementation) has KJ0 through KJ3 violation counts all 0 on these 36 cells (segment 2): every "differs from the baseline" of 1824 is an extra violation, not fewer.

**The ones that are not red, split in three under `.claude/rules/mutation-sampling.md`** (the question: if the sampling point is changed, is it still the same value):

- **1823, 1825, and 1826 (Q2's three): insensitive to the sampling point (the third class)**, not an equivalent mutation, and not a true blind spot. Basis, three points: ① the variant is effective and did change the root selection order: the KJ2 readings on KA, 18, 36, and 18, each equal the value the registration pins (`ka_pin=match`, no `invalid`), and the five locally written root selection orders in the harness read 36, 18, 18, and 52 in the segment-2 artifact (`grep -c 'name=control.*local_choice'` is 5); ② a different sampling point gives a different value: on KA (roots of the same txg and different instances, and a higher instance generation with a lower txg, can be present together in the root ring) 0 becomes 18, 36, and 18; ③ the K family builds none of these two root rings: the totals of these two columns across the 36 legal-history cells in the segment-2 artifact are both 0, and among KA's 125 states they are 48 and 24:

  The contrapositive (what would have been seen if "the K family has no sampling point" were wrong): on some cell of K, K9, or KW the variant's `root_choice_states` line or violation count differs from the baseline. In fact the three variants have `states_row_differences=none`, and the four criteria's violation counts match the baseline cell by cell across the 36 cells. This is the second reading (one side is the root ring shape the generator recorded, and the harness `assert_eq!` against the independent python generator's numbers passed; the other side is the placement the real variant code produced). The two readings point the same way. But the two share the states the generator built, and if the generator failed to build this kind of root ring both sides would get 0 together. What pins that is only the independent generator's counts and the variant readings on KA equaling the pinned values (when this kind of root ring is present, the variant goes red). Prediction t1 of registration section 4 was not refuted (registration TF4 did not fire: none of the three variants went red on the K family). Registration TF5 and TF7 fire: the three variants are not red within bounds on the K family, and at the same time they equal the pinned values on KA. Each point is written as its own number: the number of cells among the 36 of K, K9, and KW where KJ1 or KJ2 differs from the baseline is 0 (1823, 1825, and 1826 all are), and the KJ2 violation counts on KA are 18, 36, and 18 (baseline 0).
- **1824 (take the first valid one): red.** All three domains K, K9, and KW are red: KJ2 is first red at M1U0, and KJ1 is first red at M1U2 (the first time there is a confirmed publish). The mechanism is the reading of registration prediction t2 (the first self-verified slot in root ring order is the generation-0 root planted by mkfs, and within bounds it is not covered; when the chosen root is the generation-0 root, not one record is applied, and it lands on generation 0). It was not measured state by state. `differing_cells` on KJ2 is K 15/15, K9 6/6, KW 11/15, and on KJ1 is K 9/15, K9 2/6, KW 9/15. KJ3 matches KJ2, and KJ0 is 0. KA's 52 matches the pinned value. This matches the expectation of the registration 9.1 table (prediction t2). The KW half is in the section "Sensitivity to geometry".
- **1822 and 1827 (incidental)**: not red. The KA readings are 0 and 18, equal to the inferred values the registration wrote (`ka_inferred=match`; the registration did not list them among the pinned values, `ka_pin=not_pinned`). 1827 is the same as 1823: the K family has no sampling point. What 1822's registration predicts is "the same value on these four criteria and these two families": the reading is the same value, but this run did not build two slots of the same key and different content, so "equivalent on every input" was not proved. Therefore "equivalent mutation" is not written down for the record. Only "the same value on these four criteria and these two families" is written. It does not enter Q2.

**Sensitivity to geometry** (the two knobs of registration section 8, each taken at points in opposite directions):

| Whether the root ring has roots of the same txg and different instances, or a higher instance generation and a lower txg | Legal history (K, K9, KW: both columns all 0): the number of cells where KJ1 or KJ2 of 1823, 1825, and 1826 differs from the baseline is 0 for each (36 cells in all). KA (both kinds are present): KJ2 violation counts 18, 36, 18 | The verdict flips with the root ring shape (TF7): not red on the legal history, red on KA. Each point is written as its own number, and they are not combined into one conclusion |
| Whether the first slot in root ring order holds the newest root (slots per region S, and wrap) | 1824: K and K9 at S = 8, cells where KJ2 differs from the baseline 15/15 and 6/6; KW at S = 4, wrapping at txg 12 and covering the generation-0 root, 11/15 (U0 through U10 red, the four cells U11 through U14 are 0) | The verdict flips with S and wrap (TF10): the same variant is not red on the last four cells of KW |
| Journal ring slot count (in-flight limit) | 1824: KJ2 is red on every cell of both K (64 slots, limit 21) and K9 (9 slots, limit 3). `above_chosen_root_over_limit` on K9 is 0 (the baseline is also 0) | This K9 point did not act on 1824 (the last item of TF10, recorded as written): the maximum number of records above the root the variant chose is 0 on K and on K9 and 4 on KW (baseline 2, 2, 1). The limit cannot bite within bounds |
| Mount count | 1824: the KJ2 violation count is non-zero on every (M, U) cell of K's M1, M2, and M3 (the KJ2 column of `differing_cells` is K=15of15; the per-cell readings are in `cells=` on line 5 of the artifact) | It does not flip with the mount count |

The four cells KW U11 through U14: in the segment-2 artifact the counts of states in these four cells where "the first self-verified root is not the greatest one" are 1, 5, 5, and 5 (the command and the raw output follow), and 1824's KJ1 through KJ3 match the baseline on these four cells:

Registration 9.1 expects only the 4 states at U = 11 (the newest root is exactly at region 0 slot 0) to be not red. In U12 through U14, the first self-verified root of all five states is not the newest, and 1824 is still not red. What the reading can support is only this: these cells have records above the root the variant chose (`above_chosen_root_max` on KW is 4), and the placement matches the baseline. Inferred (not measured state by state): the chosen root is the txg 12 record in region 0 slot 0 after the wrap, the later records of the same instance are within the in-flight limit (21), and replay carries it back to the newest version. To refute that, look at whether `replay_applied` differs from the baseline on the states of U12 through U14, and whether the placement really equals the newest version.

**What it cannot answer (rerun 3, segment 3)**:

- Q2's "no" holds only on the K family within bounds, legal, and without faults. A newer root that cannot be read for a while and then can, a torn root slot, a dropped disk, a device lost and then found again (the shape of C331 (Root selection inversion overrides acknowledged writes)), administrator rollback, and instance switch were not swept, and the two root rings "same txg, different instances" and "higher instance generation, lower txg" are exactly what shows up in those kinds of states (D22 (Synthesizing Unit Atomicity) settled item 7 writes "a device lost and then found again builds two legal roots of the same txg"). It must not be written as "these three root selection orders have no difference" or "it has nothing to do with them": they are red on KA and have no sampling point on the K family. Building the sensitive sampling point (a legal-history family with faults) was not done this time. Building it is a new round. KA is not a legal history, and red on it does not count as Q2's "yes".
- The three variants not being red within bounds on the K family does not mean nothing is watching them: in `crates/mutations.tsv`, 1823, 1825, and 1826 each name one unit test (`of_two_self_verified_roots_with_the_same_checkpoint_txg_choose_root_takes_the_higher_instance_although_it_sits_in_a_later_slot`, `of_two_slots_with_the_same_checkpoint_txg_the_higher_instance_in_the_later_slot_is_chosen`, `a_later_checkpoint_txg_is_chosen_over_a_higher_instance_with_an_earlier_checkpoint_txg`). Whether those three catch them was not run this time (the whole crate mutation table is checker tier heavy tier, and belongs to commit time). What is said here is only that the K family of E165 (Bounded Exhaustive Recovery Replay and Mutation Kill Bound) did not catch them.
- What the variants change is the comparison expression in the two functions `choose_root_slot` (`lib.rs`) and `choose_root` (`recovery.rs`), one place at a time. Changing two or more places, changing other root selection code, and other ways of writing a variant were not swept.
- "Confirmed" on the criterion side, ideal recovery, and the independent generator are the same as segment 2, all written from the same registration text: if the text itself is wrong, they will be wrong together. The KA pinned values were computed by the script of registration section 4 (the script was not checked in; its sha256 is recorded in the registration). They match the five locally written root selection orders in the harness and the pinned values written down in the driver, three places, and the three places come from one registration text.
- The output of the driver's self-proof (67 items, eight breakage switches) has no checked-in file. The harness unit tests and harness mutations (8 rows in segment 1, 11 rows in segment 2) were not run. Until they are run at commit time, this segment's conclusion is a reading at the artifact layer. TQ13 (elapsed time) has no checked-in timing artifact, and is not reported. The frozen-root hashes at the start and the end of the driver (recovery.rs, lib.rs) are the same. `sha256sum -c` of the whole root list at the end of the segment exits 0.

### Rerun 4 (the combined-judgment family of root selection and replay with read faults, and the three root selection variants rerun 3 did not turn red)

Question sheet `e165-r4-questions.md` (Q1, Q2). Rerun preregistration `research/prompts/e165-r4-prereg.md` (including revisions 1–4; revision 4 changes only the wording of one sentence of t1, after all the artifacts). Status: **both segments have been run**: segment 1 is Q1 (not dropped inside the promise); segment 2 is Q2 (driver r4 mode, whether the three root selection variants go red on the new family: some are still not red, and all three are insensitive to the sampling point), written in the section "Rerun 4, segment 2".

The object under test is still the three steps chained in the `Consult` arm of `recover` (`choose_root`, `scan_journal`, `replay_journal(…, true)`, `crates/singlefs-core/src/recovery.rs` of 2026-10-03), not one character changed. Everything added this time is on the generator side (a trusted piece; it does not call `mount.rs` or `transaction.rs`): two system configuration slots per disk (generation number, tail, instance generation, or torn), a read fault at the start of a mount, the gate that a newer state the writable mount read cannot be read, the number-acquisition write, and the system configuration rotation at the end of a publish. **The gate takes arm A of the 2026-10-03 implementation** (one system configuration slot unreadable, or torn, is undecidable, and is taken as true; registration revision 1). That day's original text of D23 (Journal Role & Format) settled item 14 writes "undecidable takes R2; the authoritative wording is in D22 (Synthesizing Unit Atomicity) settled item 8…… the implementation here is still the 2026-09-28 arm A version, and C331 (Root selection inversion overrides acknowledged writes) is owed", so the histories generated in rerun 4 are histories that day's real write path could write. After item 7-2 of batch 7, the implementation on the root ring line takes R2 (`system_configuration_witness_is_undecidable` in `crates/singlefs-core/src/recovery.rs`). This family's arm A gate and the implementation are no longer the same. The same family under R2 is re-judged in the two steps 5-A and 5-B of the section "Rerun 5".

- **States**: one state = one in-memory disk with a root ring laid out (three region memberships 0 / 1 / 0), a journal ring (two disks mirrored), and two system configuration slots per disk, plus one "read fault at judgment time". Six domains: KF (ring of 64 slots, 8 slots per region, 1 or 2 records per publish, M ≤ 2, U ≤ 3), KF3 (k = 1, M ≤ 3, U ≤ 3), KF9 (ring of 9 slots, in-flight limit 3, M ≤ 2, U ≤ 2), and KFW (4 slots per region, root ring wrap, M = 1, U ≤ 14) are legal histories with read faults. KG takes away the two protections of the clauses (the writable mount does not judge that gate or the number-acquisition moment, and the write-row txg looks only at roots that can be read; the original sentence of one row of C331 (Root selection inversion overrides acknowledged writes), "the first publish of a new instance counts txg upward from an older root"), and is not a legal history. It is only a sampling point in the opposite direction. KA is the 125 adversarial root rings of r3, unchanged. One history = M mounts (including refused ones), one read fault at the start of each mount from mount 2 on, the publish count u_m of each mount that proceeds, the k and the commit-mark arrangement of each publish, and the crash point. One history yields 5 states at judgment time (fault at judgment time: none, RN, RI, d0, d1).
- **How faults are placed**: placements are only the three kinds root slot, system configuration slot, and a whole disk. Journal record slots do not get a read fault of their own (when a disk is absent, the record copies on that disk cannot be read either). The menu at the start of a mount from mount 2 on has 13 items: none; RN (the slot of the root with the greatest key in the root ring), SN0, SN1 (the slot with the greater generation number of the two slots on that disk), SB (SN0 plus SN1), RI (every slot of every root of the newest instance), and RNSB (RN plus SN0 plus SN1), each paired with t1 (unreadable from the first read of that placement in this mount, recovering before the reread; the reread pass, the read at the number-acquisition moment, and the reads on the write side can all be read, the R = 1 of "reread once" in D23 (Journal Role & Format) settled item 14; registration revision 4 changed the wording from "the first read cannot be read" to this sentence: on a system configuration slot placement, "only the first read is bad" is consumed by system configuration selection, the witness is not judged true, and there is no reread; what the generator writes has always been "bad until before the reread") and TP (every read in this mount cannot be read). At the start of the next mount everything can be read. A disk being absent is placed only on the one recovery being judged (a writable mount in the history refuses before it reads the disk, the device-count floor of D2 (RAID Stripe Policy) settled item 13).
- **Criteria** (each reported on its own): version = (`checkpoint_txg`, instance generation). KJ0: the three steps do not panic, and when the read view has a root, `choose_root` hands back one root. KJ1: the landed version is not earlier than the last confirmed user publish before the crash. **KJ1T** (one extra criterion of this family, on the basis of the "how to stop it" cell of one row of C331 (Root selection inversion overrides acknowledged writes), "a confirmed write that was returned can be read back on the root chosen by every later mount"): the landed version is the last confirmed version itself, or is on a descendant of it (parent chain: within one mount, the parent of each publish is the previous publish, and the parent of a mount's first publish is the version that mount used to proceed). KJ2: not earlier than any readable root in the read view. KJ3: the six projection fields of the returned root equal ideal recovery (starting from the root of the greatest version in the read view, stepping backward within the same instance, at least one copy of every record of every publish can be read, and the cumulative count does not exceed the in-flight limit). Q1's "dropped" = a state inside the promise has a violation of KJ1 or of KJ1T. Inside the promise = the history covers it ∧ judgment time covers it ∧ it is not cut off by the limit: a fault at the start of every mount in the history ∈ {none, RN, SN0, SN1, SB, RI} covers it, and the appearance of RNSB does not cover it (the two-fault shape that D22 (Synthesizing Unit Atomicity) settled item 8 writes down as not covered); a fault at judgment time ∈ {none, RN, d0, d1} covers it, and RI does not cover it (beyond a single fault, the reach of D16 (Publish Semantics) settled item 7 and settled item 8); when the clauses' ideal recovery drops the last confirmed publish because of the in-flight limit, and does not drop it once the limit is removed, it is recorded as "cut off by the limit".
- **Sources**: the gate is written from the paragraph "a newer state the writable mount read cannot be read" of D23 (Journal Role & Format) settled item 14 and from `newer_publish_witness` of `mount.rs`. The write-row txg and the counter follow note 3 of the same item. System configuration rotation follows D16 (Publish Semantics) settled item 7 and D22 (Synthesizing Unit Atomicity) settled item 16 (generation number = the greatest readable generation number of the two slots on this disk, plus one; slot = generation number modulo the slot count). Crash points cut by device follow D13 (Verification Route) settled item 4 (after the root has landed, each of the two disks' system configuration writes takes the three states not written, written, and torn, and the last mount is merged into one: the three steps do not read system configuration slots). Warm-up follows D16 (Publish Semantics) settled item 8. The acceptance of C657 (No faulty root selection combined-judgment family) is "on combined-judgment states of root selection with faults, call the real three steps; the three variants must go red, and the original implementation is judged by a confirmed complete publish not being dropped".

| Question | Conclusion | Basis (artifacts, numbers as written) |
|---|---|---|
| Q1: on a legal history with read faults, do root selection and replay together still drop a confirmed complete publish | **Not dropped inside the promise** (only within bounds, two disks, faults only at the three kinds of placement, the gate taking the implementation's arm A): the four legal domains KF, KF3, KF9, and KFW together are 11 228 080 states, of which 6 919 344 are inside the promise, and the violation counts of KJ1 and KJ1T are 0 in every (domain, M, U, fault at judgment time, history label) cell. E7RESULT name=c331_proof domain=KG local_today_kj1t_without_a_judged_fault=648 pinned=648 implementation_kj1t_without_a_judged_fault=648 implementation_kj3_without_a_judged_fault=0

The self-proof `t1_takes_the_second_read_in_the_gate_on_domain` above says: t1 of the three items SN0, SN1, and SB each has 28 466 histories in the M = 2 cell of KF that take the second-pass reread (not 0; 0 would trip TV4 of registration section 10). Here the meaning of t1 follows registration revision 4: unreadable from the first read of that placement in this mount, recovering before the reread. It is not "only the first read is bad". The two readings are not equivalent on a system configuration slot placement: the implementation's first read of a system configuration slot is in the system configuration selection step. When only the first read is bad, that read is consumed by it, the witness read can be read later, it is not judged true, and there is no reread (the four lines `timing=OnlyTheFirstRead` of `site=SN0`, `site=SN1`, `site=SB`, and `site=RNSB` in `k4t1.log`; SN0, SN1, SB, and RNSB at `timing=OnlyTheFirstRead` are all `outcome=mounted:OnTheFirstRead`). Bad until before the reread takes the reread pass (lines 3, 5, 7, and 11 of the same file are all `outcome=mounted:OnTheOneReread`). What the generator writes is the latter, so the 28 466 of this self-proof and the numbers of Q1 are both readings of the latter.

("inside_the_promise" and "outside_the_promise" are history labels: whether the fault at the start of every mount in the history covers it or not. They are not the judgment of inside the promise. Inside the promise requires all three: the history covers it, judgment time covers it, and it is not cut off by the limit. A row with RI at judgment time is never inside the promise.)

**Discriminative power and void clauses** (all in this artifact): 448 positive-control readings, `controls_not_red=0`, `void_conditions=0`, `halt_conditions=0`. `name=control` by criterion: `1 criterion=KJ0`, `147 criterion=KJ1`, `147 criterion=KJ1T`, `104 criterion=KJ2`, `49 criterion=KJ3` (each cell counts its own criteria under the three fake outcomes KF1 (the chosen root is taken as the root of the smallest key in the read view), KF2 (landing on the parent of the last confirmed version), and KF4 (landing on the root of the greatest key among the roots in the root ring truth that are not on the confirmed chain), and the readings are against the pinned values of class B in registration section 7; KJ0 uses a fake panic outcome on one designated state of KF at M = 1, U = 0 and walks the same KJ0 count, reading 1; the KJ2 readings of the six locally written root selection orders on KA, 0, 36, 18, 18, 52, and 0, are against the pinned values). The four discriminative power self-tests of registration section 8 and the self-proof of C331 (Root selection inversion overrides acknowledged writes) are all `passed=true` (lines 3303 through 3307 of the artifact). The state count of each cell, the history count, the counts of mounts that were refused and of mounts that took the reread pass, the count of states with a torn system configuration slot, the three classes the clauses' ideal recovery drops, the local root selection order readings, and the 13-item fault table at M = 2, are pinned cell by cell by `assert_eq!` in the harness against the table of class B in registration section 7 (the output of the python generator `anchors_r4.py` written separately by the designer, sha256 `5a36b72237f84c52e6901ae5affcfc5431fd4c8b123f5b9e476907b749ba1fc7`). 13 856 755 states, the same total as registration 5.4 writes.

**Sensitivity to geometry, and the trace** (the knobs of section 8, each taken at points in opposite directions): the two protections of the clauses on (KF) against off (KG): KJ1 and KJ1T violations inside the promise are 0 against 10 368 and 20 628, and TQ8's 648 appears only on the off side. Read-fault timing t1 against TP: in the fault table at M = 2, the TP of the four items SN0, SN1, SB, and RNSB has every history refused in KF (1 238 each; the summing command is the first `self_proof` line in the artifact) and 0 refused in KG. The t1 of the three items SN0, SN1, and SB (unreadable from the first read of that placement in this mount, recovering before the reread, registration revision 4) takes the second-pass reread at 28 466 each. Not one state flips inside the promise because of this knob. Fault at judgment time: the four items none, RN, d0, and d1 are 0 violations in all four legal domains, and only RI is non-zero. Mount count 2 against 3 (KF against KF3, k taken as {1, 2} against {1}): both criteria inside the promise are 0, no flip. Journal ring of 64 slots (in-flight limit 21) against 9 slots (limit 3): the maximum of the maximum number of same-instance records above the greatest root of the read view in the truth is 6 (`above_newest_root_max`), and the count of states "cut off by the limit" is 0 in the four legal domains and in KG (the 2026-10-03 implementation was not bitten by the in-flight limit on the confirmed version; the `cut_by_the_limit_states` field of every KJ1 and KJ1T line totals 0). The readings inside the promise of KF9 and of KF are the same. 8 slots per region against 4 (root ring wrap): after KFW wraps, the generation-0 root is covered, and there are 21 states at judgment time with not one root in the view (`choose_root` hands back empty 21 times, which does not count as a KJ0 violation). Inside the promise it is still 0. Threads 6 against 3: the two `E7RESULT` passes are byte-for-byte the same in full. Within bounds of this family, two roots of the same txg and different instances being present together in the root ring is 0. A pair of roots with a higher instance generation and a lower txg being present together is 0 in the four legal domains and 129 420 states at judgment time in KG (the sampling point in this family that can let a root selection variant show is only in KG, where the protections have been taken away).

Harness and root: sha256 of `crates/singlefs-checker-tier/src/bin/e165_replay_bounded_enumeration.rs` on 2026-10-03 is `4ecfa61fda6e2c0764e748533bba37abcfbed86c4c7129dac67d7f80539ac031` (17 623 lines; the first pass used `0608bda031dc90f982ddb06150079518d274ac69d773765c3acf095f1972774f`, and before the change `acd76c379479d5e9e23bc8a1fd6552c1af4f0108655c2a69c37a4ee3c2304a58`, 13 577 lines). Not one line of the generation and criterion code of the r1, r2, and r3 profiles was changed. Only documentation comments were changed and folded into an existing line, profile parsing added `"r4"` (treated as the r3 profile; the other families are the same as the r3 profile), `USAGE`, one unit test's "r4 must report an error" was replaced by "r5 must report an error", and the dispatch in `main`. The artifacts were produced by the release binary built on the root (a copy of the main workspace at the moment work started, 246 files, list sha256 `6c7d4e235597fdc2bb2f7d5aed6958e2156097b102bca3855d11a6f1bf6d05b8`; after overlaying this harness and the `crates/mutations.tsv` with 18 lines added, the list sha256 is `b5858a7aa94931c1804f4a996964aad99370360e5fc0c1658e8bc1747924febc`, and `sha256sum -c` at the end of the segment is OK on all 246). Binary sha256 `7bee4d2bb01e750a61bad2503d57a6d620a7276b513f2b9ffee6ce0b89dd34b8`. The three steps under test are that `recovery.rs` in the root (`264664af…`) and `crates/singlefs-replay-prefix/src/lib.rs` (`40cd99b5…`). `mount.rs` (`444610b4…`) and `transaction.rs` (`67d6470f…`) only had their hashes checked before the artifacts were run (TH10 of registration revision 1). The generator did not read them. Peak RSS of the whole pass is about 1.66 GB (`/usr/bin/time -v`, the two passes 1 658 480 and 1 660 708 KB; the first start hit the wrapper's memory cap at a 1G limit and exited 250, and that output does not count). The memory cap of segment 2 has to be 4G or more.

10 unit tests (newly added, in `mod root_choice_fault_tests`) and 18 harness-mutation rows (at the end of `crates/mutations.tsv`, every name starting with "E165 (Bounded Exhaustive Recovery Replay and Mutation Kill Bound) r4 segment 1"): **the unit tests and the whole `crates/mutations.tsv` table were not run** (checker tier, belonging at commit time to checker-tier-crates-mutation-replay). To show discriminative power, in a draft work tree the bodies of the 10 unit tests were called as ordinary functions by a parameter compiled only in the draft copy: the 10 unmutated ones all passed, and each of the 18 mutations was fitted with a copy that ran only the "must go red" test registered on that row, and all 18 panicked (a draft check, not a result of the gate).

**Reconciliation of the segment-1 readings with what registration section 4 predicted**: prediction s1 (the 2026-10-03 three steps land on the clauses' ideal recovery on every state at judgment time of the four legal domains, and nothing is dropped inside the promise) holds (KJ3 is 0 in every cell, and KJ1 and KJ1T inside the promise are 0 in every cell). Prediction s3 (KJ1 and KJ1T judge the same state by state on the four legal domains) holds (`kj1_and_kj1t_differing_states=0`). Prediction s4 (KJ1T is non-zero among states with no fault at judgment time on KG) holds. The reading 648 equals local ideal recovery, and the three-step placement equals ideal recovery cell by cell (KJ3 is 0). Prediction s5 (the old artifacts are reproduced byte for byte) holds (six copies, once at the start of the segment and once at the end). Predictions s2 and s6 need the variants, and are read only in segment 2. What section 4 writes, "every state at judgment time that the clauses' ideal recovery drops is in 'not covered'": KF 129 321, KF3 437 462, KF9 20 772, and KFW 44 are all RI at judgment time, the same number cell by cell as the artifact.

#### Rerun 4, segment 2 (Q2: whether the three root selection variants rerun 3 did not turn red go red on the new family, 2026-10-03)

Revision 3 of preregistration `research/prompts/e165-r4-prereg.md` (written after the segment-2 artifacts: reconciling the final sha256 of the harness and of the driver, the row numbers of `crates/mutations.tsv`, and recording one supplementary run after a memory cap was hit and the cap was raised; not one character of the criteria was changed). The harness was not changed by one character (sha256 is still `4ecfa61fda6e2c0764e748533bba37abcfbed86c4c7129dac67d7f80539ac031`, 17 623 lines). The driver `scripts/experiment-apparatus/e165-replay-mutation-kill-bounds.sh` adds a `--profile r4` mode, installed in the repository by the main agent (sha256 `3428a78ee8d41be830905e6595e3212cfa36acc49805adb45deab9f16cd06c56`, 3 365 lines; without `--profile` it is still the r2 set, and `--profile r3` is still the r3 set). The driver's `--selftest` passed all 84 items, and each of the five r4 breakage switches went red. That is the reading written when the main agent handed this segment over. There is no checked-in output file, and this segment was not rerun.

**How it was done**: on the segment-1 root (246 files), each of the six variants got its own copy and was cold-built once (found verbatim by mutation name in the root's `crates/mutations.tsv`, each exactly 1 row, the original text hitting exactly once, and the hit falling in `choose_root_slot` (`crates/singlefs-replay-prefix/src/lib.rs`) or `choose_root` (`recovery.rs`)). The baseline and every variant call the harness `--profile r4 --bounds segment1 --family root_choice_faults` (each binary, 13 856 755 states, 3 309 lines), and the violation counts of KJ0, KJ1, KJ1T, KJ2, and KJ3 and the `root_choice_fault_states` lines are compared per (domain, M, U, fault at judgment time, history label) subcell. The starting baseline is first compared line by line with the segment-1 artifact (registration TV7). At the end the baseline is built once more, and the closing line is written only when it is verbatim the same as the start (TV9). **How it was run** was started by the main agent, in two passes. The first pass was `--jobs 2 --threads 3` with a memory cap of 4G. The binary of tsv-1823 was refused by the memory wrapper (exit code 250; that output does not count as a result, TV8). The other five variants and the starting and closing baselines produced results, and the closing three lines were not written. The second pass filled in `--jobs 1 --threads 6` with a memory cap of 8G, and filled in only tsv-1823 (the other five took the per-item results saved from the first pass). So the v1824 (tsv-1823) line in the artifact was run at 6 threads, and the other five lines were run at 3 threads. That the counts do not depend on the thread count is held by the two segment-1 passes (6 and 3 threads) being byte-for-byte the same in full. It was not newly measured in this segment. The two passes' driver logs were checked in verbatim (`e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-03-r4-segment2-driver-log.out`; lines 2, 8, and 17 record the cap hit and the exit 250).

The list (the registered name uses rerun 3's number as the name. In the root and in the main workspace they are all on lines 1821 through 1826 of `crates/mutations.tsv`. The row number is only an index):
