11 in all, equal to `name=done emitted=11`. `grep -c 'halt_conditions=0 void_conditions=0 run_exit=0 run_finished=true cells_without_rows=0 abort=none copy_restored=true' <artifact>` is 6 (each of the six variant lines carries it). The baseline line (line 2) carries `result_rows=3309`, `run_exit=0`, `rows_identical_to_segment1_product=true`, and `kg_today_pin=match`. `red=1` is v1824. `not_red_within_bounds=5` is v1822, v1823, v1825, v1826, and v1827 (Q2's three plus the two incidental ones). `red_only_by_kj0_or_kj3=0`. `invalid=0 run_did_not_finish=0 not_compared=0` (all six variants were compiled, ran to the bound, and met the row-selection conditions). `ka_pin_mismatches=0` and `kg_pin_mismatches=0` (the KA pinned values 18, 52, 36, and 18, and the local root selection order readings of every KG cell, all match). `baseline_equals_segment1_product=true`.

**The ones that are not red, split in three under `.claude/rules/mutation-sampling.md`** (the question: if the sampling point is changed, is it still the same value). The basis is each one's `differs_on` and `differing_subcells` in the artifact:

- **v1823 (comparison ignores the instance) and v1825 (tie takes the lower): insensitive to the sampling point (the third class)**, not an equivalent mutation, and not a true blind spot. ① The variant is effective and did change the root selection order: KJ2 on KA reads 18 and 36, equal to the values the registration pins (`ka_pin=match`, no `invalid`). ② A different sampling point gives a different value: on KA (roots of the same txg and different instances can be present together in the root ring) 0 becomes 18 and 36. ③ The new family builds none of this kind of root ring: `same_txg_different_instance_roots` totals 0 across the four legal domains and KG (segment-1 artifact; the command is the item "the two root ring shape counts" in the section "Rerun 4"). So on the 410 subcells of the four legal domains and the 80 subcells of KG, `differing_subcells` and `differing_subcells_kg` are 0 for every criterion (`differs_on=KA`). The reading is registration prediction s2: a root slot's placement is fixed by txg alone (I-7.17 (root slot position equals the position computed from the txg)), a read fault does not move a root's position, two roots of the same txg and different instances would have to land in the same slot, and that cannot be built on an in-memory disk (inferred, not measured state by state; what was measured is that the two counts are 0).
- **v1826 (instance generation primary): insensitive to the sampling point (the third class), and the sampling point exists only in histories that have had the clauses' protections taken away.** Every criterion is 0 on the 410 subcells of the four legal domains. On KA it is 18 (`ka_pin=match`). On KG, KJ1 and KJ1T have 8 subcells each and KJ2 has 12 (80 in all) that differ from the baseline (`differs_on=KG+KA`). The sampling point is exactly "two roots with a higher instance generation and a lower txg present together": 0 in every legal-domain cell, and 129 420 states at judgment time in KG in all (segment 1). KG is not a legal history (the gate and the protection that the write-row txg looks at records were both taken away, registration section 5). Under the registration, its difference does not count as Q2's "red".
- **v1827 (the glue spreads the key without the instance generation): insensitive to the sampling point (the third class)**, the same as v1823: every instance generation the core sees is 0, which equals "comparison ignores the instance". KJ2 on KA reads 18 (the value inferred in the registration, `ka_pin=not_pinned ka_inferred=match`). The legal-domain and KG subcells are all 0 (`differs_on=KA`).
- **v1822 (a tie takes the later one as the winner): the same value on this family and on KA. In the three-way split it is recorded as insensitive to the sampling point (inferred), and not as an equivalent mutation.** The four legal domains and the KG subcells are all 0. KJ2 on KA reads 0 (`differs_on=none`, `ka_pin=not_pinned ka_inferred=match`): none of the six domains has an input that makes it differ from the baseline. It chooses differently from the original expression only when "root ring order has two self-verified slots whose choose-newer keys (txg, instance generation) are exactly the same and whose contents differ". A legal history has only the three copies of the generation-0 root as the same key and the same content (neither this family nor KA built two slots of the same key and different content), so within bounds there is no sampling point. It is not that "the same value on every input" has been proved. The unit test named by the row in `crates/mutations.tsv` that carries `m1_tie_last_wins`, `of_two_slots_with_the_same_choice_key_the_earlier_slot_is_chosen`, has a name that writes this cell. The unit-test body was not read in this segment and was not run (checker tier). To turn it from "inferred" into "measured", one cell of two self-verified slots with the same key and different content has to be added (that takes a new round).

**Reconciliation of the segment-2 readings with what registration section 4 predicted, and with the failure clauses of section 10**: prediction s2 (v1823, v1825, and v1826 are not red within bounds on the four legal domains, third class of the three-way split; v1826 has a sampling point on KG, and v1823 and v1825 have one only on KA) holds: all three are 0 on the 410 legal subcells, v1826 is non-zero on KG, and the other two are 0. Both also match registration section 4 and 9.1. Failure clauses: TF5 (Q2's "some are still not red") fires. Each of the three is recorded as the third class, and each writes whether KG has a differing cell (v1826 does, the other two do not). TF6 (prediction s2 refuted) did not fire: none of the three legal domains is red, and at the same time both root ring shape counts are all 0. TF7 (the positive controls did not match) did not fire. "7 `kill_bound` lines" in TV3 holds (the artifact's `variants=7`, 7 `name=kill_bound` lines; the `--list-items` cell is an item of the driver's self-proof, and this segment was not rerun). #### Rerun 5, segment 2 (3-B: mutation kill bound, 2026-10-04)

The driver `scripts/experiment-apparatus/e165-replay-mutation-kill-bounds.sh` adds `--pins r5` (each of the three modes has its own set of pinned values: the sha256 and the function ranges of `recovery.rs` `4454f6d3…`, replay-prefix `lib.rs` `9966f42f…`, and `crates/mutations.tsv` `bc2627f8…` on the segment-1 root; the V7 / TV7 comparison targets are changed to the segment-1 rerun artifacts; without `--pins` the original three sets are not changed by one character). What ran was the copy at sha256 `164e04b1e72aa1f6acf4b663137fc88d1ff5dbb0a431ac2a9a893ae4b049c64e` (after the main agent installed it in the repository, `--selftest` was 99 items with 0 failing, and each of the two breakage switches went red; these two readings have no checked-in output file). After that the driver filled in, only for 5-B, the two pinned values of the `--gate r2` set and the name of the harness's gate field (sha256 after the fill-in `904777afe38473aec51208dcd98b60eca2552f70e418c85443206b36fbc2287c`): the difference between the two versions is entirely in `R5_GATE_R2_PINS`, `R4_GATE_R2_CONFIG_FIELD`, the two branches in `check_the_registered_inputs` that recognize only a placeholder string, and the self-proof. The code the three modes walk when `--gate` is not given did not change. The hand-made table's Z5 had its anchor replaced in place (name, meaning, and replacement text unchanged; the original text changed from the no-longer-present `unmount_marker: UnmountMarker::NotWrittenByTheUnmountSequence,` to `unmount_marker: record.new_unmount_marker,`, hitting exactly once in `crates/singlefs-core/src/recovery.rs`; sha256 of the hand-made table after the anchor change `f2803173b5004e332a62b3191335e2853ed7058bfbfd7cca5e7fd29753a64bc9`). All three modes ran on the segment-1 root (`--jobs 2 --threads 3`; memory cap 8G for r2 and r4 modes, 1G for r3 mode). All four exit codes are 0. All three copies wrote the closing line 「已还原，基线与开头逐字相同」. Of the 1 373 baseline lines of r2 mode, 1 240 lines are found verbatim in the two segment-1 r2 profile artifacts (`rows_found_verbatim_in_segment1_products=1240`). #### Rerun 5, segment 3 (3-C: the r5 profile and j8, 2026-10-04)

The harness adds `--profile r5` (it recognizes only the three families legal history, adversarial, and multi-disk): the generator writes per D23 (Journal Role & Format) settled item 15, "when one publish is cut into several records, every record carries the same new-root segment". Every record of publish j carries the instance-table pointer, the allocation record tree root, and the unmount marker of version R_j, timeline version j. Records in the adversarial-family alphabet, and residues in domain R, carry the function of the distinguishing number d (instance-table pointer slot 200 000 + d, allocation record tree root slot 300 000 + d, and the unmount marker takes the other value of the chosen root R_0). The generator records each record's "new-root-segment projection → the last three items" as the truth, and stops on the spot if the same projection has two different copies of the last three items (halt clause PH6). The five j8 cells (registration 5.3): **J8a**, one line each for the instance-table pointer, the allocation record tree root, and the unmount marker — on a state that has been applied (the returned root's projection ≠ the chosen root's projection), this field of the returned root must equal the same field of the record whose new-root-segment projection in the truth equals the returned root's projection; **J8b** — the returned root's fsid equals the chosen root's; **J8c** — on a state where nothing was applied, the four items fsid, instance-table pointer, allocation record tree root, and unmount marker of the returned root all equal the chosen root's. The generators, criteria, and output of profiles r1 through r4 were not changed by one character (PV3). The harness was changed on the main workspace through `scripts/hosts/compile-then-swap.py` (`d6937857…` → `f72fc1a4…`). `crates/mutations.tsv` gained 9 lines (lines 2414 through 2422, names carrying the words "r5 segment 3").

The new cells of 3-C against the old cells (the correspondence table of registration 5.3: J8a instance-table pointer ↔ J7a instance-table pointer, J8a allocation record tree root ↔ J7a allocation record tree root, J8a unmount marker ↔ J7b, J8b ↔ J7a fsid, J8c ↔ J7c; the old cells are taken from the checked-in artifacts of rerun 2, matched by (family, parity, domain, N)), the five `name=pq9_files` lines and the line `name=pq9_summary` in `e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-04-r5-segment3-cell-correspondence.out`, in full:

830 cells (five new cells for each (family, parity, domain, N)). Every cell has the new cell and the old cell both 0, and "0 / non-zero" is the same cell by cell. j8 is 0 in every cell on the implementation (PQ8, PF4 did not hit). States where "j8 has no source" are 0 in every cell (PQ10, J8a has no state it under-judged). The verdict line of the legal-history family, domain L, N ≤ 10 (the line `name=verdict domain=L ring_slots=64 smallest_n=1 largest_n=10` in `research/results/e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-04-r5-segment3-r5-legal-family.out`, in full):

Discriminative power of the parity: C8b is 142 under `even` and 620 under `odd`. A threshold moved to sit between the two points (for example 400) makes `even` red and leaves `odd` unmoved. "The r1 generator column is 0" is the other half: the same fake outcome is invisible on a generator whose three record items are constantly the r1 constants. C8e (two copies of the last three items for the same projection must stop at PH6) is a unit test, and was not run (checker tier).

**Void clauses** (`e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-04-r5-segment3-profile-check.out`): PV2 (the r5 profile against the r2 / r3 profile on the same command, compared line by line after removing the j7 / j8 fields, the `config` line, and the profile-name text) has `differing_lines=0` on all five pairs. PV3 (eight reruns of profiles r1 through r4 on the segment-3 binary, against the same-named segment-1 artifacts) and PV5 (the r5 profile legal-history and adversarial families at 7 and at 3 threads), verbatim (lines 22 through 31, in full):

#### Rerun 5, segment 5, step 5-A (Q1 (R2): after a system configuration read error takes R2, does a legal history with faults drop anything, 2026-10-04)

Registration revision 1 (the main agent ruled item 2: the family with faults under R2 is folded into this rerun). The harness adds `--gate r2` (recognized only under `--profile r4 --family root_choice_faults`; without it, arm A is taken, and the r4 profile output is unchanged byte for byte). The generator's gate is changed to the reach of D22 (Synthesizing Unit Atomicity) settled item 8: a writable mount reading the system configuration witness is **undecidable ⟺ this pass's read view has a system configuration slot that reports a read error, and no disk has both slots readable and not torn**. At the number-acquisition moment, (a) undecidable refuses, and (b) if no disk has one copy that is readable and not torn, it refuses (the same reach, "when no disk has one self-verified system configuration, the number-acquisition step also refuses"). A tear is read as "readable, not self-verified, and the device does not report a read error". A system configuration slot placement on the fault menu at the start of a mount is read as "the device reports a read error" (the main agent ruled item 2 of revision 1; the basis is the S-distinction sentence of the reach of D22 (Synthesizing Unit Atomicity) settled item 8 and `system_configuration_slots_of_one_device_read_once` in `crates/singlefs-core/src/recovery.rs`: a read error is recorded only when it cannot be read, and readable but not self-verified is recorded at neither end). The three steps under test still do not read system configuration slots. R2 only changes which histories are refused. At the start of the work, the nine rows of the "generator's gate checked against the implementation, place by place" table in revision-1 section 3 were re-checked by function name on the segment-1 root, and all nine rows match (row 7, the per-disk lagging-arm check, is not built, as the registration says, and is inferred unreachable). The harness was changed onward after segment 3 (`f72fc1a4…` → `8006b251…`). `crates/mutations.tsv` gained another 13 lines (lines 2423 through 2435, names carrying the words "r5 segment 5"). A whole pass is 182 seconds at 7 threads and 360 seconds at 3 threads, and the two passes are byte-for-byte the same in full.

The three that are not red are still judged **insensitive to the sampling point (the third class)**: the variants are effective (`ka_pin=match`), they take a different value once moved to KA (v1826 also has KG), and the two root rings in the four legal domains of the R2 family that could let them show (same txg and different instances present together, higher instance generation and lower txg present together) are 0 in every cell (PQ24 of the 5-A artifact). The closing line and the verdict line (lines 9 and 10 of the artifact): `已还原，基线与开头逐字相同`; `E7RESULT name=verdict scope=segment2 variants=7 variants_excluding_baseline=6 red=1 red_only_by_kj0_or_kj3=0 not_red_within_bounds=5 invalid=0 run_did_not_finish=0 not_compared=0 ka_pin_mismatches=0 kg_pin_mismatches=0 baseline_equals_segment1_product=true gate=r2`.

**How the two passes were checked against the sentence that did not match**: on the first pass the memory cap was 8G (`E165_KILL_BOUNDS_MEMORY_CAP`), and the binary of tsv-1812 (v1824) was refused by the memory wrapper (exit code 250; that output does not count as a result, revision-1 section 11). The driver exited 1. The closing line reported 「还有 4 个变体没有行：tsv-1812 tsv-1813 tsv-1814 tsv-1815」. On the second pass the memory cap was 16G. The start reported 「已有行 3 个；这一次要跑 1 个：tsv-1812」, and only that one was filled in. The two sentences do match. The reason is in how the driver is written: each variant, once it has finished and has no defect, saves its own per-item record, but the lines in the artifact are flushed in variant order, and it stops when one earlier in the order has no result. On the first pass tsv-1812 hit the cap. tsv-1813, tsv-1814, and tsv-1815, which come after it, finished, saved per-item records, and were not written into the artifact, so "no line" counts the 4 missing from the artifact. On the second pass "lines already present" counts the three lines already in the artifact, baseline, tsv-1810, and tsv-1811. What has to be run is the 1 that has neither a line nor a per-item record. After tsv-1812 finished, tsv-1813 through tsv-1815 were flushed into the artifact from the first pass's per-item records. It was checked one by one which pass each of the seven lines came from, and whether it is an exit-0 result: each variant's timing (compile, run, and total seconds) is the same in three places, the per-item record, the driver log, and the progress record. baseline, tsv-1810, tsv-1811, tsv-1813, tsv-1814, and tsv-1815 are the first pass (8G). tsv-1812 is the second pass (16G). All seven lines carry `run_exit=0 run_finished=true cells_without_rows=0`. `problem` in the per-item records is empty. The baseline at the start of the second pass is verbatim the same as the baseline line recorded in the artifact (if it differed, the driver would exit 6 and not go on). So not one line has to be voided under a void clause. The two passes' driver logs and timings were checked in verbatim (`…-first-pass-*`, `…-second-pass-*`). The progress record (one line per variant, including the first-pass tsv-1812 line `problem=内存包装退出码 250，这一次不算结果`) was checked in verbatim as `…-progress-log.out`. The artifact names follow the `--output` the driver received, verbatim (what the registration wrote is `segment5-r2-gate-kill-bounds.out` and `segment5-r2-gate-driver-log.out`; different names were used when it ran, and the contents did not change). ### Rerun 7 (adversarial root ring placed by the placement formula: KA replaced by KL, 2026-10-23)

Rerun registration `research/prompts/e165-r7-prereg.md` (L1 through L4 of question sheet 1.2; section 12 revisions: none). Cause: the placement check of C743 (rebuilt allocator recording new class does not verify root occupies slot) went into the implementation (`root_sits_in_the_ring_slot_the_formula_gives` in `crates/singlefs-core/src/root_ring.rs`, and `read_root_ring_slot` in `crates/singlefs-core/src/recovery.rs`: a root that passes its self-verification but does not live in the slot the formula gives is read as a bad read of that slot). The roots in the r3 profile's adversarial root ring KA (slots 0, 1 and 2 of region 0 each take empty or one of four roots, 125 states) do not live in their placement slots, so all of them are read as misplaced reads, and KA lost its object (registration section 4, 4.1). The user decided to "place the roots by the placement formula": KA was replaced by a new domain KL, in which the placement slots of txg 100, 101 and 102 (the "slot within the region = (txg div R) mod S" of D22 (Synthesizing Unit Atomicity) settled item 16, region = txg mod R, R = 3, S = 8: the 2nd, 9th and 17th slots in root ring read order, which hold txg 102, 100 and 101 in that order) each take empty or one of the two roots of instance 1 and 2 of that txg, 3³ = 27 states; the other root slots and the journal ring are all 0, and no generation-0 root is placed; it goes through the real three steps `choose_root`, `scan_journal` and `replay_journal(…, true)`. The wording of the KJ2 criterion is unchanged; on KL it judges two things: a root with a higher instance generation and a lower txg does not win over a root with a higher txg, and an older root earlier in root ring read order does not win over a newer root later in it. Two roots of the same txg cannot both be present, so the "tie between different instances of the same txg" half has no object (not judged, which is not the same as judged as 0). The name KA stays with the old r3 definition; the old artifacts and the drivers on frozen roots still read it as KA.

Status: **segment 1 has been run**; the artifact half of L1 and L2 are enough to judge. The inline-test half of L1 (the inline test command of registration 5.8: the changed and the newly added inline tests) and the five harness mutation lines added to `crates/mutations.tsv` this time are checker tier and were not run; they are left to the main agent. L3 (segment 3: the readings of the six root selection order variants on KL) and P1b (the 3-thread differential) were not run after it was enough to judge. L4 was enough to judge at design time (among the 360 data lines of the pinned red-example table `.claude/gate.d/e165-pinned-red-examples.tsv` not one line is a domain of the root selection family; the segment-1 runner counted them, and the table was not changed).

The harness was changed in place (registration 5.6, H1 through H8): KL of the r3 and r4 profiles comes from the same generator function `landing_slot_adversarial_root_rings`, and every root it places is checked back against the implementation's `target_for_publish` by the local placement; `--only KA` exits 2 for both families. After the patch was applied the harness sha256 is `25a298d80f6e89f2d1ddaaca2da82529ee7887dabfe0a5227cbab0f8ca6a5240`. The three artifacts use the same binary (sha256 `ce552d8df472e3c566a5ef6a51e4a4c014f15ac35628f4d96cc01856ca95f541`, built with the patch applied on a copy of the main workspace's `crates/` at that time; no root list was kept for that copy), 31 threads, memory cap 8G, and all three passes exited 0. Peaks recorded by the memory wrapper: P1 545 259 520 bytes, P2 1 787 822 080 bytes, P3 3 336 568 832 bytes.

| LQ2 today's three steps on KL | KJ0 violations 1 (the state with the root ring all empty, where `choose_root` returns none), judged 27; KJ2 violations 0, judged 26; the same in all three. L1 does not flip | The two `name=violations domain=KL` lines of each of the three |

The slot-reading differential over the 27 states gives `slot_readings_differing_from_the_truth=0`: every root lives in its placement slot, and the placement check blocked none of them. In the two faulted artifacts the KJ1 and KJ1T violations of KF, KF3, KF9, KFW and KG and the 10 368 and 20 628 within KG's commitment are non-zero as before and byte-identical to the comparison targets (LQ5 = 0); what they mean is read from the two sections of rerun 4 and of step 5-A of rerun 5, and this run did not judge them again.

### Replay

`bash scripts/experiment-apparatus/replay.sh E165` (bounded exhaustive recovery replay and mutation kill bound): (registration lines `driver_e165`, `driver_e165_light`, `driver_e165_adversarial`, `driver_e165_segment2`, criterion exact). The counts do not depend on the thread count: segment 1 `--family legal` was run once each at 10 threads and at 7 threads, and `--family adversarial` once each at 16 threads and at 7 threads, and the `E7RESULT` lines are byte-for-byte the same.

The segment-2 driver `scripts/experiment-apparatus/e165-replay-mutation-kill-bounds.sh`: `--list-items` lists 26 variants. Each artifact line carries the command that replays only that one (`--recheck --item <variant>`, and after the rerun the new line is compared with the line in the artifact). If it breaks in the middle, rerunning the same command fills in only what has not been run. Each line of the red-example ledger carries the single-state replay command `target/release/e165_replay_bounded_enumeration --replay-state <domain>:<N>:<ordinal>`, and the verdict of the replay on the original implementation is column 12 of that line. On 2026-10-01 the whole segment took 32 minutes at `--jobs 4 --threads 4`, with load averages on the same machine of 17–90 (verbatim in the segment-2 timing artifact).

The segment-2 artifacts of rerun 1, `e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-01-r1-segment2-kill-bounds.out` and `e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-01-r1-segment2-red-examples.tsv`, were produced by the r1 version of the same driver. In the replay registration, the segment-2 line (the line starts with the experiment number, and after that, in order, `@driver_e165_segment2`, an empty parameter column, the artifact name, and the criterion) pointed at them before 2c of rerun 2, and afterward was changed to point at `@driver_e165_segment2||e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-02-r2-segment2c-kill-bounds.out|exact`. Those two r1 copies are kept. The sha256 of `crates/mutations.tsv` pinned by the r1 driver is `8c24d2c627caf36cd0931a5b0405c19c0a7f24a7086d2b0e9ede1fd58431fe38`. Today's driver is the r2 version and is written only for the r2 profile, so it cannot reproduce them (to reproduce the r1 numbers, use the r1 copy of the driver in git and the r1 frozen root). The segment-2 artifacts from before the move (`e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-01-segment2-kill-bounds.out`, `research/results/e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-01-segment2-red-examples.tsv`, `e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-01-segment2-kill-bounds-timing.out`) cannot be reproduced on today's tree and are kept, not deleted. The three lines of segment 1 (`driver_e165`, `driver_e165_light`, `driver_e165_adversarial`) are left where they are: the two families of the rerun are byte-for-byte the same as the checked-in copy.

The six lines added by rerun 2 (one new file for segment 3, `@driver_e165_r2_segment3_multi_disk`, and five lines for segment 4, `@driver_e165_r2_segment4_table_both_legal`, `…_table_both_adversarial`, `…_table_both_multi_disk`, `…_reversed_disk_order_legal`, `…_reversed_disk_order_multi_disk`) are all `--profile r2`, with the thread count pinned at 8. The counts do not depend on the thread count (the multi-disk family at 8 threads and at 4 threads is byte-for-byte the same in full). How they are done, and where they come from, is written in the comments of those driver functions in `scripts/experiment-apparatus/replay.sh`. The legal-history family and the adversarial family of segment 4 each take about 200 seconds (8 threads, load averages on the same machine 25–35). The driver of the segment-2 supplement of rerun 2 (2c), `scripts/experiment-apparatus/e165-replay-mutation-kill-bounds.sh`, requires `--root` to point at the frozen root (the table hash the driver pins is the frozen root's `9d5fd9fc…`; if `crates/mutations.tsv` in the main workspace has changed, it stops at RH1). The whole segment is 12 batches, about 2–3 minutes for each batch of two variants (load on the same machine 12–30). The `@driver_e165_segment2` line in `replay.sh` was changed to point at its artifact. Starting the whole segment at once (not split into batches) is estimated at 15 to 20 minutes (inferred, and a whole round was not measured; that segment2b pass took 778 seconds for the whole round, of which five variants exited in 0.5 seconds; this pass runs all 26 variants, two in parallel inside a batch, twelve batches of 2–3 minutes each). This line is a heavy tier replay, run by the main agent only at commit time.

Rerun 5: segment 1 changed lines 228 through 234 of `replay.sh` to point at segment 1's rerun on the segment-1 root (the old artifacts of rerun 2 and rerun 3 are kept; the harness of 2026-10-04 cannot reproduce them on the two J7a cells). Lines 224 through 226, 235, and 237 are left where they are (the rerun is byte-for-byte the same as the checked-in copy). Ten registration lines and ten driver functions were added (how they are done, and where they come from, is written in the comments of those driver functions in `scripts/experiment-apparatus/replay.sh`): three lines for segment 2, `@driver_e165_r5_segment2_r2_mode_kill_bounds`, `…_r3_mode_kill_bounds`, `…_r4_mode_kill_bounds` (the driver uses `--pins r5`, `--root` points at the segment-1 root by default, and the function writes how to rebuild the root if it is gone; r2 and r4 modes are 8G, r3 mode is 1G, `--jobs 2 --threads 3`); five lines for segment 3, `@driver_e165_r5_segment3_legal`, `…_adversarial`, `…_multi_disk`, `…_odd_marker_legal`, `…_odd_marker_adversarial` (main workspace `cargo run`, `--profile r5`, thread count pinned at 8); one line for step 5-A, `@driver_e165_r5_segment5_r2_gate_root_choice_faults` (main workspace `cargo run`, `--profile r4 --family root_choice_faults --gate r2`); one line for step 5-B, `@driver_e165_r5_segment5b_r4_gate_r2_mode_kill_bounds` (the driver uses `--pins r5 --gate r2`, `--root` points at the step 5-A root by default, memory cap 16G). The artifacts of segment 3 and of step 5-A were produced by binaries built on the segment-3 root (harness `f72fc1a4…`) and the step 5-A root (harness `8006b251…`). The harness in the main workspace on 2026-10-04 is `8006b251…`. The step 5-A binary, rerun on the step 5-A root, is byte-for-byte the same in full as the legal-history copy of segment 3. The other four copies were not rerun with it. When they cannot be reproduced, check against the root list first. All four of the driver lines are heavy tier replays, run by the main agent only at commit time.

Rerun 6: one registration line was added, E165 (Bounded Exhaustive Recovery Replay and Mutation Kill Bound) `|@driver_e165_r6_r3_legal||e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-09-r6-r3-legal-family.out|exact`, and the driver function `driver_e165_r6_r3_legal` (main workspace `cargo run`, `--bounds segment1 --family legal --profile r3`, thread count pinned at 8; how it is done, and where it comes from, is written in the comment of that function in `scripts/experiment-apparatus/replay.sh`). The artifact was produced by a binary built on the root of rerun 6 (list sha256 `56d52a1c…`). If `crates/` in the main workspace changes later, and it cannot be reproduced, check against the root list first. The r2 legal family on the same root is only a comparison target, and no replay line is added for it.

Rerun 7: the three registration lines of E165 (Bounded Exhaustive Recovery Replay and Mutation Kill Bound) in `scripts/experiment-apparatus/replay.sh`, `@driver_e165_r3_segment2_root_choice`, `@driver_e165_r4_segment1_root_choice_faults` and `@driver_e165_r5_segment5_r2_gate_root_choice_faults`, now point to this run's three artifacts (`e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-23-r7-r3-root-choice.out`, `e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-23-r7-r4-root-choice-faults.out` and `e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-23-r7-r5-gate-r2-root-choice-faults.out`); the driver commands did not change (main workspace `cargo run`, thread count pinned at 8; the three counts do not depend on the thread count, registration 5.8); the three old artifacts of segment 2 of rerun 3, segment 1 of rerun 4 and 5-A of rerun 5 are kept. So from this run on, the `<artifact name>` in the replay commands of those segments of reruns 3, 4 and 5 is the new artifact of rerun 7. Reproducing them needs the harness in the main workspace with the rerun-7 patch applied (sha256 `25a298d8…`); the three artifacts were run with a binary built on a copy of the main workspace's `crates/` at that time, and no root list was kept for that copy, so when a reproduction does not match, check the harness first and then look for changes elsewhere in `crates/`. The five mutation kill bound drivers run the old harness on frozen roots, where KA is still the old definition; this run did not touch them.

Assertions that compare them joined together: path 1 against path 2 is j4 (`judge_a_legal_state`, from line 1941); path 3 against path 2 is S's own comparison (line 2028); path 4 against path 5 is `assert_the_pinned_i812_state_counts` (an `assert_eq!` cell by cell for every (domain, n); the harness does it on every run, and all ten cells match). Paths 4 and 5 share the written form of the alphabet and of the predicate: if both sides read the same predicate wrong, they go wrong together and still match cell by cell. What pins that is the classification counts of class B in section 7 of the registration, plus the four cells of the KF3 fake outcome (the first call returns the chosen root, the second call reports an error, and it does not read the implementation). The three paths share the generator and the same batch of clauses: if the generator builds them wrong, or the clauses are read wrong in all three places together, the three paths go wrong together and still match cell by cell. What pins that is the state counts and the witness-count anchors of section 7 of the registration (computed by another python script) and the positive control. The values come from sweeping the whole domain within bounds, not from enumerated points.

Segment 2 of rerun 3: path 6 against path 7 is KJ3 (`judge_the_outcome`, from line 10240: the six projection fields of the returned root against the six fields of that version of ideal recovery). KJ1 and KJ2 are the version path 6 landed on, against the truth the generator recorded (the last confirmed publish, and the root of the largest version in the root ring). The number assertions of path 7 and path 8 are in `assert_the_pinned_root_choice_cell` (an `assert_eq!` item by item for every cell; the harness does it on every run, and all 37 cells match). Paths 6, 7, and 8 share the generator and the registration text: if the generator, how a confirmed publish is recognized, or the version order is read wrong, the three go wrong together and still match cell by cell. What pins that is the positive control (the readings of the two fake outcomes KF1 and KF2, and the readings of the five local root selection orders on KA, which do not read the implementation) and the counts of the independent generator. The slot-reading differential (`root_slot_reading_agrees_with_the_truth`, line 10343) compares the reading of `read_root_ring_slot` with the truth at generation time, slot by slot. It is one independent assertion between the layer where the three steps read the disk and the generator. The values come from sweeping the whole domain within bounds (every crash point in every cell), not from enumerated points.

Segment 3 of rerun 3: the two routes of path 9 match variant by variant (18, 52, 36, 18), but no assertion joins them directly. The comparison of the local copy against the pinned-value constants is in the harness (the positive control's `must_see=equals_…`, checked on every run). The comparison of the real variant's reading against the pinned value is in the driver (`ka_pin`; if they differ, the whole round exits 1). The two sides are joined indirectly by the same set of pinned values. The two routes share the KA generator, the criterion of KJ2, and the comparison text of registration 9.1: if the generator or the criterion is read wrong, the two go wrong together and still match cell by cell. What pins that is the script in section 4 of the registration that computes the pinned values (it does not read the harness). The K-family half of Q2 (the variant compared with the baseline cell by cell) is two runs of the same binary family compared with each other. The baseline is line by line the same as the segment-2 artifact (TV7). Beside it, what is pinned is the state counts and several counts of the 36 cells of segment 2. The values come from sweeping every state of K, K9, and KW within bounds, and the 125 states of KA, not from enumerated points.

Segment 1 of rerun 4: path 10 against path 11 is KJ3 (the six projection fields of the returned root against the six fields of that version of ideal recovery), KJ1, KJ1T, and KJ2 (the version landed on, against the truth the generator recorded: the last confirmed publish, the parent chain, and the roots that can be read in the read view). It is written in `judge_a_state` (from line 14176) and `count_the_outcome` (from line 14417), and the harness does it on every run. The number assertions of paths 11 and 12 are in `assert_the_pinned_fault_cell` (an `assert_eq!` item by item for every cell; all 49 cells match) and `assert_the_pinned_mount_two_table_under_the_gate` (13 items in each of the four domains). Paths 10, 11, and 12 share the same registration text: how the gate is taken (the implementation's arm A), the txg of the written row taking the minimum the clauses allow, which placements the read faults sit on, and how the three labels are recognized. If that is read wrong, the three go wrong together and still match cell by cell. What pins that is the positive control (the readings of the three fake outcomes KF1, KF2, and KF4, and of the six local root selection orders, which do not read the implementation; on the one cell M = 2, U = 3 of KG, the two columns of KF4 differ), the discriminative power self-test of C331 (Root selection inversion overrides acknowledged writes) on KG (the 648 computed by local ideal recovery and the 648 the implementation's three steps read on the same cells are the two routes each computed once), and the slot-reading differential (`fault_slot_reading_agrees_with_the_truth`, line 13863: a placement faulted at judgment time must read as `Unreadable`, and a slot that reads as self-verified must equal the truth). The values come from sweeping the whole domain within bounds (every crash point and every fault in every cell), not from enumerated points.

Segment 2 of rerun 4: the two routes of path 13 match variant by variant (KA: 18, 52, 36, 18, and 0 for v1822, 18 for v1827; KG: the KJ1, KJ1T, and KJ2 total of each (M, U) cell against that local column, `ka_pin_mismatches=0`, `kg_pin_mismatches=0`), but no assertion joins them directly. The comparison of the local copy against the pinned-value constants is in the harness (the KJ2 readings of the six local root selection orders on KA, in the positive control, checked on every run). The comparison of the real variant's reading against the pinned value is in the driver (`ka_pin`, `kg_pin`; if they differ, the whole round exits 1). The two sides are joined indirectly by the same set of pinned values. The two routes share the generators of KA and KG, the criteria of KJ1, KJ1T, and KJ2, and the comparison text of the six replacement lines of registration 9.1: if the generator or a criterion is read wrong, the two go wrong together and still match cell by cell. What pins that is the python script in section 7 of the registration that computes the local root selection orders (it does not read the harness). The legal-domain half of Q2 (the variant compared with the baseline subcell by subcell) is two runs of the same binary family compared with each other. The baseline is line by line the same as the segment-1 artifact (registration TV7). The values come from sweeping every state of the four legal domains within bounds, and every state of KG and KA, not from enumerated points.

Segment 3 of rerun 5: path 1 against path 14 is the five cells of j8, written in `judge_the_fields_beyond_the_projection_against_the_generated_records`, and the harness does it on every run. The two routes share the generator: if the generator writes the last three items wrong (for example, writing back the r1 constants), the record and the truth are wrong together, and j8 is still 0. What pins that is the pinned values of the fake outcomes of C8a through C8d (762, 620, 142, 2 348, computed separately from "the three items outside the projection of version j are pairwise distinct, and the unmount marker alternates in parity", lines 62 through 78 of `research/results/e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-04-r5-segment3-r5-legal-family.out`) and "the r1 generator's column is 0", and the assertion that the r5 profile state count equals the r3 profile state count. The values come from sweeping every state of the three families within bounds, not from enumerated points.

Step 5-A of rerun 5: the two routes of path 15 match cell by cell through `assert_the_pinned_fault_cell_under_the_gate` and `assert_the_pinned_mount_two_table_under_the_gate` (49 cells, 49 lines of the three refusal-reason columns, 52 lines of the fault table of mount 2, an `assert_eq!` item by item; the harness does it on every run). The two routes share that stretch of text in revision-1 section 5: if the text itself writes R2 wrong (for example, whether a tear should count as reporting a read error), the two go wrong together and still match cell by cell. What pins that is the five discriminative power self-tests (the first one takes different values at the two points arm A and R2), the 13 lines of harness mutations of revision-1 section 9 (not run), and the check at the start of the work that the generator's gate was compared with the implementation place by place at nine places. Paths 10 and 11 stay as they were (the three steps, ideal recovery, and the four criteria) and judge on the history generated under R2. The values come from sweeping the whole domain within bounds, not from enumerated points.

Rerun 6: the two routes of path 16 match one by one (3 110, 762, 2 348, 70; the line `Y8 anchors_r6_rerun=` in `e165-replay-bounded-enumeration-2026-10-09-r6-comparison.out`, and those 23 lines of Y8), but no source assertion joins them directly (the script was not checked in). On every run the harness judges red by comparing the comparison readings with the local constants. The two routes share the text of the input domains of the original registration 5.1 and 5.2: if the text itself writes the domains wrong, the two sides go wrong together and still match one by one. The values are every state inside the comparison bound (domain L with N ≤ 5, and the whole e1 bound), not enumerated points.

Rerun 7: the two routes of path 17 are joined in the harness: `assert_the_pinned_landing_slot_adversarial_shape` (`assert_eq!` on each of the six: state count, root ring all empty, same txg with different instances, higher instance with lower txg, two or more instances, the first one read is not the newest; done on every run of the r3 and r4 profiles), and the comparison readings against the pinned values (`must_see=equals_…`, checked on every run); in the three artifacts the six and the comparisons equal the pinned values cell by cell (LQ1, LQ3). The two routes share the wording of the placement formula, the root ring read order and the version order: if that wording itself is read wrong, both sides go wrong together and still match cell by cell. What pins it is the check of the local placement against the implementation's `target_for_publish` (done for every root placed) and the slot-reading differential (`slot_readings_differing_from_the_truth=0` over the 27 states: a root placed in the wrong slot would be read as misplaced by the implementation's placement check, and the number of states with the root ring all empty would go from 1 to 27). Path 6 (the implementation's three steps) judges KJ0 and KJ2 on KL as before. The values sweep all 27 states of KL, not enumerated points; the reading that takes only the 9 states of txg 100 and 101 (registration B5) was not run.

### What it cannot answer

- **Rerun 7's "KJ2 violations 0 on KL" holds only on these 27 states of KL**: three txgs (100, 101, 102), two instances per txg (1, 2), S = 8, no generation-0 root placed, journal ring all empty. A tie between different instances of the same txg has no object on KL (the placement formula keeps two roots of the same txg from both being present), so that half is not judged, which is not the same as judged as 0. Ties between the several byte-identical generation-0 roots (the one cell of ties still left in D22 (Synthesizing Unit Atomicity) settled item 7) were not built in this family. v1822, v1823, v1825 and v1827 reading 0 on KL is an equality, not a comparison; the variant-arm comparisons for them are carried instead by the crates tests named in `crates/mutations.tsv` (registration item 4, waiting for the main agent to accept it); the real readings of the six variants on KL (L3, segment 3) were not run after it was enough to judge. The harness inline tests (changed and newly added) and the five harness mutation lines added this time are checker tier and were not run; the 3-thread differential P1b was not run after it was enough to judge, and "the counts do not depend on the thread count" carries over the old readings cited in registration 5.8. The binary for the three artifacts was built on a copy of the main workspace's `crates/` at that time, and no root list was kept.
- **Rerun 6's "the r3-profile legal family comparison ran, all red" holds only for the one cell of even unmount marker, direct table build, and normal disk order**: the r3 legal family with odd unmount marker, both tables taken, and reversed disk order was enough to judge but was not run afterwards (the r3 profile has no official run of these); "differs from r2 on the same root only in the profile name" was compared only on this one cell. The new unit and the two mutation lines were not run; "the j7 row can only be caught by the new unit" and "the J6Qn row also goes red on the two r5 units" are both inferred, measured by the three commands of registration 9.3.
- **Rerun 5 3-C's "j8 is 0 in every cell" holds only within this bound and this one way of choosing values**: the three items outside the projection were swept in only one way (on the timeline the three items of version j are pairwise distinct and the unmount marker alternates in parity; outside the timeline they take a function of the distinguishing number); the ways in which the two instance table pointers are the same, the allocation record tree root is always empty, or the unmount markers are all of the `Written…` kind were not swept; a publish whose several records carry different last three items (a legal write path cannot write it) was not built; `--table both`, reversed disk order, the faulted and root-selection families, and more than two disks were not run on the r5 profile. The generator both builds the records and records the truth, so when the generator is wrong j8 is still 0; the only thing pinning it is the fake-outcome pinned values of C8a through C8d.
- **Rerun 5 3-A's "the judgment changed, and only in j7" does not show the implementation is wrong**: j7 judges the application rule that was superseded by the original text of settled item 15 of D23 (Journal Role & Format); 3-A compares only the judgment fields, and the rows where the judgment is unchanged but the count changed are reported separately (the `name=pq2*` rows of the first comparison artifact).
- **Rerun 5 3-B's "the kill bound did not change" compares only the first-red position and the outcome**: after going red, whether other criteria go red at larger N was not measured; the list of criteria red at the N of first red (J7a's two extra cells) is reported separately and does not count as a flip.
- **What rerun 5 5-A and 5-B (the R2 family) did not build**: for a tear, only one reading was taken, "readable, fails self-verification, does not report a read error" (the reading where a tear also reports a read error has the same value in this family as "the first half of R2 removed", and serves only as a mutation); the mixed form where the system configuration slots read back all zeros, both slots of one disk reporting read errors at once (the mount-start fault menu has no such item), the per-disk check of the lagging branch (inferred unreachable), a read error persisting until the next mount, and the "only the first read is bad" kind of t1 were not built; the bound and placement are the same as in rerun 4. Q1 (R2) "nothing within the commitment is lost" and Q2 (R2) "some are still not red" speak only inside this bound. The peak of the whole R2-family pass was not read (the wrapper did not record a measured peak for that run), and why tsv-1812 exceeded 8G on the first pass of 5-B was not investigated.
- **Rerun 5's harness unit tests and harness mutations were not run**: the 16 newly added unit tests and the 22 mutation lines at `crates/mutations.tsv` lines 2414 through 2435 (9 lines whose name carries "r5 segment 3", 13 lines whose name carries "r5 segment 5") are checker tier; registrations PV9 and PV10 are judged by the output of checker-tier-crates-mutation-replay at commit time; until then the discriminative power of 3-C and 5-A rests only on the comparison readings in the artifacts and the discriminative power self-tests. Segment 4 (PQ13: the first red of the four application-broken variants on the r5 profile, and the positive control of j8 on real code) was not run after it was enough to judge, so whether j8 catches the real variants was not measured.
- **Rerun 4 segment 1 Q1's "nothing within the commitment is lost" holds only within this bound and these placements**: two disks, region ownership 0 / 1 / 0, 4 and 8 slots per region; read faults land only on three kinds, the root slot, the system configuration slot, and the whole disk (journal record slots, two or more read faults within one mount (other than SB, RI, and RNSB themselves), and faults that are not recovered across two or more mounts were not built); M ≤ 3 (KF3, k = 1), U ≤ 3 for KF and KG, U ≤ 2 for KF9, U ≤ 14 for KFW, at most 2 records per publish; the journal ring does not wrap; faults on system configuration slots at judgment time (the three steps do not read them), the crash points of the write that takes the number, the remaining system configuration fields and F, admin rollback, instance switch, publish failure and verbatim resend, switch to read-only, and root selection of a read-only mount were not built.
- **Rerun 4's gate takes arm A of the 2026-10-03 version of the implementation** (registration revision 1): that run's "nothing within the commitment is lost" holds only for histories generated under arm A. After 7-2 the implementation takes R2, and a history where "one system configuration slot is unreadable while the other disk's two slots both self-verify" is no longer rejected and goes on; the same family under R2 (same bound and placement) was rejudged by rerun 5 5-A, which answers "nothing within the commitment is lost"; the shapes it did not build are written in the rerun 5 items.
- **The "only the first read is bad" family was not enumerated** (registration revision 4, code round three verdict K4-a): t1 on this page is "unreadable from the first read of that placement in this mount, recovering before the reread". With only the first read bad, the read of the system configuration slot is consumed by selecting the system configuration, which may select the other slot and go down another path afterwards; not one history of this family was built; Q1's "nothing within the commitment is lost" does not cover that family. The owed check is recorded by the main agent ("record the owed check, do not build" in the verdict).
- The generator's written row txg takes the minimum the clauses allow (the largest txg of the roots and records readable on the descent pass, plus one), while the implementation takes the root read by the third direct disk read, which may be larger; the read faults of this family do not touch record slots, so a state where the two differ cannot be built (the latest root and all records of that publish being unreadable together can only be told apart by t1).
- The three "within the commitment" labels are readings the registration set according to the clause text: RI is not shielded at judgment time (making all roots of one instance unreadable at once exceeds a single fault), RNSB is not shielded in the history (the double-fault shape that settled item 8 of D22 (Synthesizing Unit Atomicity) states is not shielded), RI is shielded in the history (C331 (Root selection inversion overrides acknowledged writes) took arm A for this); changing the reading changes the "within the commitment" state counts. KJ1 and KJ1T judge identically state by state in the four legal domains, so this family cannot tell the two readings apart; on KG the two are told apart (18 684 states). KG is not a legal history, and its numbers do not flip Q1.
- **Rerun 4 segment 2 Q2's "some are still not red" holds only within this bound and these placements** (same as the segment 1 Q1 item: two disks, region ownership 0 / 1 / 0, faults on only three kinds of placements, the gate takes the implementation's arm A): the variants change only the comparison expressions in the two functions `choose_root_slot` (`lib.rs`) and `choose_root` (`recovery.rs`), one place at a time; other ways of writing were not swept. It must not be written as "these three root selection orders make no difference" or "unrelated to it": v1823, v1825, and v1826 go red on KA, and v1826 also goes red on KG; the four new legal domains have no sample points for them; "the root slot placement is determined only by the txg" (I-7.17 (root slot position equals the position computed from the txg)) is an inferred reading, and what was measured is only that the two root ring shape counts are 0 in the legal domains. The root rings that would make them red violate I-7.17 (root slot position equals the position computed from the txg) (KA), or remove the clause protection (KG); those two cells are not legal histories, and their red does not count as Q2's "red". The acceptance of the C657 (No faulty root selection combined-judgment family) row was "the three variants must go red", which was not met this time (none of the three goes red in the three legal domains); how to handle this row is left to the main agent.
- v1822 (tie goes to the later one) has the same value as the baseline in all six domains: "the sample points are not sensitive" is inferred (two self-verifying slots with the same key and different contents would give different values; neither this family nor KA built them), not measured, and it is not recorded as an equivalent mutation on file.
- Rerun 4 segment 2 was generated under arm A (registration revision 1), so that run's Q2 readings hold only for the set of histories under arm A; the same family under R2 was rejudged by rerun 5 5-B (some still not red, same as rerun 4). How to handle the C662 (The legal history family with faults is still generated by taking A) row is left to the main agent.
- The v1824 (tsv-1823) row was a 6-thread supplementary run, the other five rows were run with 3 threads; the output of the first pass, where 4G hit the ceiling (exit 250), is unused. The output of the driver's `--selftest` (84 items, each of the five r4 break switches red) has no checked-in file, and this segment did not rerun it; the whole unit-test and harness-mutation table was still not run (checker tier, belongs to commit time); whether the unit tests named on `crates/mutations.tsv` lines 1821 through 1826 catch them was not run this time, and all that is said here is that E165 (Bounded Exhaustive Recovery Replay and Mutation Kill Bound)'s new family did not catch them.
- The whole unit-test and harness-mutation table was not run (heavy tests of the checker tier); the equivalence check on the draft copy proves only that the unit-test bodies go red, and TV5 is judged by the checker-tier-crates-mutation-replay run at commit time.
- Out-of-bound inputs: N above each domain's upper edge, one record naming multiple units, an empty publish record, more than two instances, and overwriting that goes around the ring more than once were not swept (the last paragraph of registration 5.1). "0 violations" speaks only inside the bound.
- Records with sequence number 0 in the adversarial domain: `parse` rejects them and they cannot enter the table, so S's (e) "sequence number 0" cannot be reached on the built table; how a reader should judge bad records other than sequence number 0 is not in this alphabet.
- Segment 2's "N of first red" is the first N that differs from the baseline line by line, without direction: a variant with fewer violations is also recorded as red (the Z1 row of "line-by-line judgment" Q13). Once a domain goes red it is not looked at further up; whether other criteria go red at larger N after going red was not measured.
- Rerun 2 segment 2 supplementary run (2c), the second half of Q1, compares only the first-red N of j6 and J6Qn (the text of the line against the same line of the baseline), without direction; in the nine cells where j6 is red and J6Qn is not red inside the bound, the j6-red half is j6 recording violations of the kind "second top-of-range error" (form one is where the original implementation has it and the variant does not, form two is where the variant adds it), and this does not say the variant's defect is caught by j6 in this cell. The frozen root is not a git repo, and the two `git status --porcelain crates/` at the start and end of the driver both printed only "fatal: not a git repository" with identical empty output, so `crates_git_status_unchanged=true` has no discriminative power on this root; whether `crates/` changed during the run is covered by `sha256sum -c` (222 lines at the start and end of the segment, exit 0) and by the driver's recovery.rs and lib.rs hashes being identical at start and end. The raw `what` of the second call was not captured (the red-example ledger stores only per-state lines).
- Line 448 does not go red inside the bound; whether it is a real blind spot, an equivalent mutation, or sample-point insensitivity, the registration says is not judged in this experiment (F18).
- The red-example ledger's direction looks only at the first red state; whether the whole domain has more or fewer violations was not counted separately.
- S clause 3 (b) "meets a record below the watermark on the chain" takes "stop": the clause does not say it verbatim, the main agent inferred it on 2026-10-01. Whether this cell is reachable in the legal-history family was not counted separately.
- j6 measured only one reading, "fixed point"; the other three readings of K7 are not in the harness.
- Q6's same-paragraph item B, "split rows by stopping in the record segment / stopping in the root slot segment", cannot be told apart literally (cut B has no separate root slot segment); what the harness reports is the two rows "whether the root-slot write takes the same value on two states"; whether that is enough is for the main agent to decide.
- Besides the three callers written in registration 5.3, `replay_journal` in `crates/` is also called from the checked-in harness `crates/singlefs-checker-tier/src/bin/e158_root_choice_repair.rs` (listed in registration section 13); whether the three callers differ in anything besides the boolean switch and the reader was not checked line by line (stop clause H5 was checked only as far as grep).

## Historical Versions

### 2026-10-23: Rerun 7 segment 1 (adversarial root ring placed by the placement formula: KA replaced by KL)

Registration `research/prompts/e165-r7-prereg.md` (revisions: none). After the placement check of C743 (rebuilt allocator recording new class does not verify root occupies slot) went into the implementation, all 125 states of the r3 profile's adversarial root ring KA were misplaced reads; the user decided to place the roots by the placement formula, and the harness replaced KA in place with a new domain KL (the placement slots of txg 100, 101 and 102 each take empty or one of instances 1 and 2, 27 states), with the r3 and r4 profiles coming from the same generator function. The three root selection artifacts (r3 profile, r4 profile, r4 profile with `--gate r2`) were produced again. The artifact half of L1 is enough to judge: the shape of KL is 27, 1, 0, 7, 12, 4, today's three steps give KJ0 1 / 27 and KJ2 0 / 26, and the local root selection orders give 0, 0, 6, 0, 4 (the r4 profile adds tie goes to the later one, 0), equal cell by cell to registration section 7 in all three and byte-identical across them. L2 is enough to judge: every line outside KL is byte-identical to the previous artifact, and the summary line differs only in the state count, by −98. The inline-test half of L1 and the five harness mutation lines added this time were not run (checker tier); L3 (segment 3) and P1b were not run after it was enough to judge; L4 was enough to judge at design time. Added the section "Rerun 7", a paragraph in "Replay" (the three registration lines in `scripts/experiment-apparatus/replay.sh` now point to the new artifacts, and the three old artifacts are kept), path 17 in "Path and conclusion registration" and the rename of path 13's local function (`ka_kj2_violations_of_a_local_rule` → `kl_kj2_violations_of_a_local_rule`), and one item in "What it cannot answer"; reviewed the twelve rows of "Decisions affected" line by line, and the relations are all unchanged.

### 2026-10-04: Rerun 5 (after the record format was changed along root ring line 7-2; the faulted family after a system configuration read error takes R2)

Registration `research/prompts/e165-r5-prereg.md` (including revision 1: adding segment 5). ### 2026-10-03: Rerun 4 registration revision 4 (wording of the t1 reading, wording only)

Registration `research/prompts/e165-r4-prereg.md` gained revision 4 (timed after the two segments of artifacts; the original criteria are kept as they were): t1 changed from "the first read of that placement in this mount is unreadable" (in parentheses, "the first pass of the read phase is unreadable, the reread pass ... is readable") to "unreadable from the first read of that placement in this mount, recovering before the reread". The two sentences are not equivalent on the system configuration slot placement: the implementation's first read of a system configuration slot is in the step of selecting the system configuration, so when only the first read is bad that read is consumed by it, the witness is not judged true, and no reread is taken (lines 2, 4, 6, 10 of the code round three attacker's `k4t1.log`, `OnTheFirstRead`); when bad until before the reread, the reread pass is taken (lines 3, 5, 7, 11, `OnTheOneReread`), and the generator wrote the latter. The one sentence of t1 for SN0, SN1, and SB in this page's "how faults are placed" and "sensitivity to geometry and trajectory", the explanation of the self-proof `t1_takes_the_second_read_in_the_gate_on_domain`, and "What it cannot answer" were changed in step; the artifacts, harness, and Q1 and Q2 conclusions and numbers were not changed by a single character. When only the first read is bad, selecting the system configuration may select the other slot and go down another path afterwards, and this family was not enumerated. The eleven rows of "Decisions affected" were reviewed line by line, and the relations are all unchanged.
